Explain the reasons!” “Resting the mind is the great essential point,” he said. I asked if establishing others in Dharma for fame and disciples isn’t a good thing to do? He said, “this is like the sound of ringing a hand bell with the strength of a white lion. Your fame is also like this. If your relationship with disciples is like Lokeshvara’s motivation of old, then it’s that way,” he said. Then I prostrated. I prayed, “may the Buddhadharma in general, and particularly the accomplishment lineage of the Taklung Kagyu Dharma grow and flourish! And especially, may the MarthangTaklung Dharma of Riwoche grow and flourish! May I be endowed with the good fortune to be able to establish in Dhamra all the regions of Do Kham.” Saying “may it become like this,” I snapped my fingers three times. Then afterwards, Lilavajra spoke three times: “What is your connection with Yarjon today?”Hearing these unpleasant words, I asked “what is the point of that?” “There is no point,” he replied. “Well then, I have a powerful reliquary with the the amrita of Krsnari and the authority of the Dharmapala of the Tent.” He gave the torma empowerment of the Great Powerful Mandala of Krsnari. For the secret initiation, he assigned HerukaTrinlay Dupa Tsal with two hands holding varja and ghanta. Bestowing the daily practice text and so on of HevajraKrsnari, he said “benefit beings!” Then I said, “I don’t think I’ve received Rinpoche’s supplication!” “What does this evil person say? I have one answer, that the Rosary of Kagyu is self arisen from marble.” Then bestowing the self-arisen Dharmapala of the Tent from the Black Rock Charnel Ground, and others, he said “this is to stabilize your faith.” Thus Je Choktrul Rinpoche, on the twenty-eighth day of the eighth month of his fifteenth year, a prophesy arose during a life accomplishment, excellently wrote this afterwards.</small> ^11-14

## 12. Knowing the Space of Objects of Knowledge ^12-0

Vast mandala of those who know the space of knowledge
Brilliant deeds bestowed on the realm of disciples,
Illuminating all the doctrines of the Victorious Ones,
I prostrate to the Precious Kagyu! ^12-1

Abandoning the eight unfavourable states, perfectly obtaining the freedoms and endowments,
attaining supreme accomplishment, master of those who practice virtue,
Regent of the Sage, crown ornament of the Land of Snows,
I prostrate to peerless Gampopa! ^12-2

Perfect Guru, precious protector of beings,
coming to Shanti Mountain for the benefit of disciples,
acting for the benefit of others through the Dharmas of Scripture and Realization,
I prostrate to You, Regent of the Teacher! ^12-3

From his birth in the female earth sheep year onward,
until coming to the Place of Miracles,
all of your limitless, completely wonderful history
I will briefly recount, please listen! ^12-4

Lotus Guru Pushpachandra,
witnessed by the Buddha, you became “Youthful Moonlight.”
becoming a Bhikshu-Physician at this snow mountain retreat,
I prostrate to You, who hold the Victor’s prophesy! ^12-5

Born in the region of Nyal consistent with your aspiration,
you married a Lady in keeping with the customs of the world,
You bestowed unlimited healing medicine for the benefit of others,
I prostrate to You, human embodiment of Bodhicitta! ^12-6

Then seeing that Samsaric actions have no essence,
You left home and were ordained to the Dharma,
guarding the Three Trainings like your own eyeballs,
I prostrate to You, Master of Bhikshus! ^12-7

Placing the dust of the Holy Teacher’s feet on your head,
You learned vast teachings on Vinaya, Mantra and so on
Developing innumerable samadhis, attaining the dreamlike Bhumis,
I prostrate to You, Precious Merit! ^12-8

When you heard the Guru’s name through pure aspiration Bodhicitta,
With hairs quivering, You experienced supreme increasing joy,
You experienced signs in dreams and gained perfect qualities,
Fortunate person, I prostrate to You! ^12-9

When you came before the Venerable Milarepa,
He said, “You, precious for all sentient beings,
I consider to be my heart son and lineage heir!”
I prostrate to You, Teacher from Central Tibet! ^12-10

He gave the oral instructions for the supremely profound, Holy Path of Means,
With no distraction, You diligently practiced and accomplished
And realization, the Guru’s kindness, was born in Your mind,
I prostrate to You, diligent one! ^12-11

You entered the supreme Path of Secret Mantra
You reached the limits of the transmission of the qualities of the Ten Signs,
and became qualified to work with channels, nadis and bindu,
I prostrate to You, Lord of Yogins! ^12-12

When meditating on the four-session yoga of the Two Stages,
You met the Seven Medicine Buddha Brothers in the Sambhogakaya Pureland,
And met the Lord of Sages and the other Thousand Buddhas,
I prostrate to You, Mount Meru, Lord of Humans! ^12-13

You proclaimed praises for the sake of the Venerable Master!
Realizing the Three Kayas, attaining the Two Siddhis,
You thus attained the state of Bhikshu Vajra Holder.
I prostrate to the Venerable One, famous throughout the world! ^12-14

Completely transmitting all empowerments and Dharma without exception,
abandoning evil companions and embracing solitude,
Accomplishing the benefit of others, saying “Protect your stock of accomplishments!”
I prostrate to You, Great Being! ^12-15

When staying in Olka, realizing that all Dharmas like dreams,
and unreal, manifesting the state of Simplicity,
You said “this is my last rebirth!”
I prostrate to You, Lord of the Ten Bhumis! ^12-16

When you arrived at the place of DaklaGampo,
Keeping in mind to accomplish according to the Guru’s instructions,
You spoke the Dakinis’ prophecy for the benefit of others,
I prostrate to the Lord of Dharma Gampopa! ^12-17

Synthesis of all manifold fortunate, karmically favored ones,
You taught the Guru’s oral instructions, holy and profound,
You ripened and liberated limitless gathered beings
I prostrate to the peerless supreme Guru! ^12-18

For a time, you showed the four manifestations of Nirmanakaya,
Consecrating and teaching the Dharma to others,
flying through space, and staying in sealed retreat,
I prostrate to You, with power over all appearances! ^12-19

When training in the mental practice of profound Samadhi,
overpowering the elements, expanding and transforming,
Your stainless body emanated as Lord of the World,
I prostrate to You, miraculous Supreme Being! ^12-20

When staying among innumerable learned and accomplished ones,
overpowering others’ perceptions through the courage of Dharma,
You said, “I am Lord of the Doctrine!”
I prostrate to You, lion among humans! ^12-21

When meditating on the Illusion-like Samadhi,
You manifested the Great Enlightenment of Inflated Space,
and You showed unimaginable miracles,
I prostrate to You, inseparable appearance and emptiness! ^12-22

All those with faith saw you as Chakrasamvara,
transforming consecration into Great Enlightenment,
The Jnanasattvasactually dissolved into the Supports,
I prostrate to You, who achieved supreme accomplishment! ^12-23

All people with faith, wherever they may be,
just simply by seeing, hearing, and remembering You,
abandoning suffering, attain the Path of Liberation!
I prostrate to You, King of Physicians! ^12-24

When practicing the Mudra of the Method of Joining Amrita,
the three of body, appearance, and mind rub together and merge into one,
Your illusory body instantly emanates uncountable forms,
I prostrate to You, Royal Supreme Guide! ^12-25

When the thought arose in your mind to pass away for the benefit of others
On the fifteenth day of the sixth month of the female water bird year,
Your great being departed to the Dharmadhatu.
I prostrate to You, who passed into Nirvana! ^12-26

Many rainbow lights with parasols, and victory banners, and
rainfalls of flowers, and the sound of music occurred.
All the earth shook, and all the birds cried out,
I prostrate to You, amazing miracle! ^12-27

Innumerable people endowed with faith gathered,
presenting exquisite offerings of gods and humans,
You bestowed heart and tongue relics on those with faith,
I prostrate to You of altruistic deeds! ^12-28

Luminous Dharmakaya, naturally without elaboration,
Sambhogakaya, the natural appearance of Great Bliss,
Nirmanakaya ripening and liberating all beings,
To the Supreme Guide, the Guru with Your lineage, ^12-29

I offer the Cloud of Offerings of Samantabhadra, equal to space!
I individually admit all non-virtues of body, speech and mind!
I rejoice in all the virtue of myself and others!
I pray you to stay forever, turning the wheel of Dharma! ^12-30

Protector, when You were showing Enlightenment,
Even though you gazed at all beings with the eyes of compassion,
may I and all disciples not let it diminish,
Please satisfy us with the amrita of the Supreme Vehicle! ^12-31

The Guru’s feet remaining firm throughout all existence,
By making offerings to accomplish profit and honor,
and always remaining inseparable from the Teacher’s perception,
May I never be clothed by faulty thinking, ^12-32

And acting in the same manner as the deeds of the Guru,
Making supreme offerings with wonderful devotion,
and in all actions, not thinking of the faults of others,
May I continually accomplish beneficial thoughts! ^12-33

By my realizing enlightenment like Yourself,
For all sentient beings who equal space that become disciples,
Making a festival of Dharma with mantra and confidence,
May the sound of the Mahayana Dharma ring out! ^12-34

By prostrating and offering, confessing, and
Rejoicing after others, and exhorting supplication,
Whatever small amount of virtue I have accumulated,
not abiding in the extremes of existence, nonexistence, and so forth,
personification of the Four Kayas and Five Jnanas,
I dedicate all to unsurpassable complete Great Enlightenment! ^12-35

<small>So, this prayer, based on the biography of the complete liberation of the Lord of Dharma, Glorious Gampopa, along the with Seven Branches, was written at the Great Seat of Glorious Phagmo Drupa by the Venerable Phagmo Drupa himself.</small> ^12-36

Possessing the prophesy of the Tathagata,
You benefitted beings in these degenerate times.
You mastered the Mahamudra
I prostrate to Youthful Moonlight! ^12-37

Glorious Phagmo Drupa, in a forest retreat,
Ripening many fortunate disciples,
You showed various inconceivable miracles.
I pray to the precious protector of beings! ^12-38

<small>This was written by Ngawang Namgyal.</small> ^12-39

## 13. This is the Prayer Based on the Biography of the Protector of Beings Phagmo Drupa called the Pleasant Rain of the Amrita of Blessings ^13-0

Homage to the Guru! ^13-1

Long ago, the Tathagata Krakucchanda (Destroyer of Samsara)
In this realm, the Completely Enligtened One, Shakyamuni
In the future, in the world “Covered with Flowers”
You take the form of Akshobhyavajra, and teach the Dharma,
I pray to the Precious Protector of Beings! ^13-2

In the sight of the Buddha Unattached Lotus
the householder Wheel of Glory and so on,
the Eight Masters who attained the final limit of bodhicitta (the Tenth Bhumi)
learned and accomplished king’s ministers who manifest purified ordinariness,
I pray to the guide of limitless sentient beings! ^13-3

Then, as the Regent of Youthful Moonlight,
in Do Kham, toward Dri Valley, by the Five Clairvoyances
manifesting the Nirmanakaya, without the suffering of birth,
in that very moment, clearly knowling the three times,
I pray to the Holy Supreme Nirmanakaya! ^13-4

Arising from the ocean of innumerable Samadhis,
In front of the Kalyanamitra Blazing Ethics, and
Victory Banner of Light Rays, becoming ordained,
You became known as the Protector of Beings, Dorje Gyalpo
I pray to the one who wears the crown of the Signs of Perfection! ^13-5

Rest from noise and labor, principal one of the many, and so on,
Not relying on training, naturally becoming omniscient,
from the Sixteen Spiritual Friends from Do Kham,
You listened to inconceivable authoritative texts and oral instructions,
I pray to the one who ripens and liberates the fortunate! ^13-6

At the age of 22, you went towards U Tsang (Central Tibet)
and coming to the Dharma College Seat at the edge of Tolung,
You listened to many texts of Vinaya, Abhidharma, Madhyamaka, and Prajnaparamita,
Without an equal in teaching debate, exposition, and composition,
I pray to the Lord of the Learned in the Land of Snows! ^13-7

At the Dharma College of Tsurphu, the great Tamer of Actions and so on,
from the masters and disciples of the Assembly of the Learned,
becoming fully ordained, holding the victory banner of the burial shroud,
and became famous as the second Buddha,
I pray to the one who beautifies this world! ^13-8

From teachers of the Mahayana, Ja Yul and so on,
You took the vows of Aspiration and Application Bodhicitta,
Before the face of Tsechen Kunga Nyingpo
You listened to an ocean of mantra, tantra and oral instructions,
and manifested the warmth and signs of channels, nadis, and bindu,
I pray to the Great Vajra Holder! ^13-9

The Bodhisattva Chandradhvaja, and
Tsami Lotsawa, Palchen Galo, and so on,
from innumerable learned and accomplished Masters,
all the Dharma precepts that exist in this Valley of Snows without exception,
were heard, contemplated, and meditated, ripening your mind stream,
I pray to the Great Learned and Accomplished One! ^13-10

Prophesied by the Victor, peerless Gampopa’s
lotus feet were placed on your head, offering the three ways of pleasing,
You actually perceived the unmistaken truth of the Dharmata,
applying the elegant amrita of profound vast certainty,
You vowed to equal the Guru’s abandonment and realization.
I pray to You, Great Dharma King! ^13-11

In pure vision, meeting the Holy Buddha and
the One Mother, andfilled like a vase with all the healing
oral instructions that could be obtained, without exception,
in the forty-ninth Male Earth Tiger Year,
in the forest solitude of Samantabhadra, you accomplished Varahi.
I pray to the one who abides on the Lion Throne! ^13-12

Those who thirst for the amrita of Brahma’s speech,
fortunate disciples, in numbers beyond counting,
abide in the discipline of the amazing holy Dharma,
Surrounded by a throng of Noble Ones, who guard the precepts,
Taming and pacifying with the Bodhicitta of exchanging self and others,
I pray to the supreme Great Sattva! ^13-13

Completely delighted by the vajra dance of great bliss,
attaining complete enlightenment by the royal samadhi of evenness,
pervading by continuous inexhaustible activity,
Gaining power over the ocean of innumerable Dharma treasures,
by the natural state of bliss and emptiness, Mahamudra,
I pray to the one who ripens and liberates the fortunate! ^13-14

Of the twenty-five chief disciples, five hundred umbrella-bearers,
and an assembly of eight hundred, and others beyond number,
There is no one who was not liberated just by hearing the profound Dharma.
Heart essence of all the Wheel of Dharma,
with perfect words and perfect meaning, equal to space,
I bow down to those who composed the commentaries that protect the Dharma, ^13-15

whose Three Secrets manifest inconceivable miracles,
and the smallest connection accomplishes great benefit for beings,
Guru who knows the capacities of each fortunate one,
I pray to the fully accomplished one,
who manifested twelve times 108,000 bodies. ^13-16

Innumerable disciples of this place were liberated from the ocean of existence,
and in the sixty-first Tiger year,
on the twenty-fifth day of the ripening moon, your manifested Form Kaya
dissolved, and in the stainless pure land,
in the presence of the Buddha Akshobhya, attained enlightenment.
I bow down to LodroDrimay Gyalpo! ^13-17

When your remains were purified, the Tathagatas of the Five Families appeared,
and the assembly of Dakas and Dakinis praised and made offerings,
heart, tongue and eye relics beyond imagination,
with earthquakes, rains of flowers, and massed rainbows,
a holy place with a Stupa of Dharmakaya was established.
I pray to the Inconceivable Miracle! ^13-18

Thus, by seeing, hearing, and remembering this meaningful
King of Dharma, the great protector of beings,
if one prays while keeping your life story in mind,
from the equanimity of the Dharmata, great loving ^13-19

compassion, like masses of clouds,
for those who follow your teachings, Protector,
on the fertile ground of the mine and other disciples’ mind streams,
Please allow the gentle rain of the amrita of blessings to quickly fall,
and ripen the perfect harvest of the Four Kayas of Great Bliss! ^13-20

King of Dharma, in your Dharma Assembly,
overpowering hordes of Maras who hold malicious wrong views,
clearing away all outer, inner and secret obstacles, without exception,
please increase the ocean of those disciples who rest equally
in the definitive meaning of the natural state of bliss- emptiness! ^13-21

In all countries, may the continuity of seasons be peaceful,
may everyone, everywhere, practice the path of the Ten Virtues as one,
And may all together quickly attain
the Holy State of the Vajra King! ^13-22

<small>So, the Vajra Master, named Ayu, of the Great Dharma College, DrupgyuTengye Ling, that holds the Long Tradition of Definitive Meaning, requested me, Trinlay Jampa Jungne, who has received a small amount of blessing from the Venerable Guru, the Great Protector of Beings, to write this. May it be excellent virtue!</small> ^13-23

## 14. This is the Praise of the Life Story of Glorious Taklung Tangpa called the Great Drum of Sincere Faith ^14-0

Venerable Lord of the Four Kayas, Vajradhara,
I bow at the feet of glorious Taklung Tangpa!
Generally, whatever perfect knowledge there is,
arises, Lord, from Your compassion itself. ^14-1

For that reason, all your followers and disciples
this prayer is the royal Single Sufficient Dharma.
The prayer that gives birth to certainty
comes from perceiving the life story of the Father Guru. ^14-2

The great waves of this life story, profound and secret:
for Him, except for the joy of the Noble Ones,
How could there be any experience of self-fixation?
As soon as a little of the ultimate fundamental meaning is spoken. ^14-3

In the pure realm of the highest citadel (Akanistha),
is the one called the Sixth Buddha, Dharmakaya Vajradhara.
On the peak of Potala Mountain,
is the protector Avalokiteshvara, Lord of the World. ^14-4

In the glorious western pure land of Orgyen,
is the Lord of the Supremely Accomplished, Wangpo’i Lo
In the eastern pure land called Stainless,
is the Lord Sambuddha, the Protector Akshobhya. ^14-5

Generally, in all the limitless pure lands in the ten directions,
the noble assembly of the Lord Victors with their children,
those all are the Lord of Dharma Guru’s
display, the miraculous emanations of his embodied form, ^14-6

and for my, an ordinary disciple’s perception,
In this pure land of the Lord, King of the Shakyas,
In this northern portion of the glorious, enduring Jambudvipa,
He came, manifesting a form like a reflection, ^14-7

to put things briefly in a pleasant way,
without any faults of exaggeration and denigration in the telling,
Then, from your mouth, Lord Jewel,
came speech like the great roar of a lion: ^14-8

My country is the prosperous lower Tang Kya,
My family lineage is the Three Great Clans of the White-Blazed Onyx,
My father is the radiant Monk Minister,
My mother is the Wrathful Lady of Three Virtues, ^14-9

I myself am inseparable from the Body, Speech,
and Mind of the Buddhas of the Three Times,
which has been asserted by the Victors of the Three Times.
Reading writings and so on, and training and practicing as taught,
I was ordained at the Glorious Tangkya Monastery. ^14-10

From Khenpo Gaton, the temple caretaker,
From Lopon Tsarton at the Gomang College,
At the Drak Gon in Guge and so on,
Hearing the Dharma, many misconceptions were resolved. ^14-11

From the Great Siddha, the Yogi of Lato,
I requested the Three Lamps of the Path of Pacification,
At that time, the Dharma Lord, the Protector of Beings,
just hearing his name caused admiration and devotion to blaze ^14-12

Earlier, when I went to U, I made many plans,
however, those were delayed and remained unfinished, and
at that time, the Precious Nirmanakaya,
joining with Lord Golpu Kyen,
Like a vulture soaring through the sky,
came to U without any obstacles. ^14-13

From the forest retreat of the Glorious Perfect Phagmo Drupa,
I saw the sacred place of the Dharma Palace of Yang Gon,
Immediately on seeing it, my body hairs quivered
I wondered if all the concepts had ended!
Very quickly, I came into his presence, ^14-14

and beheld the Tathagata abiding on a throne
I offered rare and precious gold dust,
I prostrated with reverence, and inquired after his health,
Like I was his child, he immediately answered with delight. ^14-15

Your coming here with no obstacles
was due to my compassion, he said.
Even when I went to Vajrasana at Bodhgaya,
You said that you must follow after me. ^14-16

To the west of there, all the disciples
Recently, when new disciples arrived,
their beginner’s bodies newly broken,
“Dissolve into my heart!” you said ^14-17

By bestowing one Guru Yoga practice,
All supreme and ordinary accomplishments, without exception,
are understood to arise only from interest and devotion. ^14-18

Better than offering a mandala of an ounce of gold,
He told me, for one practicing like a yogin,
Practicing the meaning by interest and devotion is sufficient,
Many different Dharmas are not necessary ^14-19

the Precious Lord said joyfully,
interest and devotion have the power to restore well-being.
Moreover, not only in this life,
from beginningless time we have had a profound connection
Son, the arising of your good qualities
depends on me alone, he said. ^14-20

From our ancient connection, our minds have merged into one,
So now also, offer mandala again and again,
supplicate one pointedly, and
many different good qualities will arise. ^14-21

I, the Master of the Teachings,
Son, so that certainty will arise in you,
Hold and guard whatever Dharmas you have requested,
Then later, when the necessity arises, ^14-22

Through prophesy, empowerment, blessing, and
authoritative oral transmissions, profound instructions and advice,
all Dharmas fill you completely like a vase,
and with the essential points of reverence and devotion, ^14-23

you will attain the ultimate path.
By actually beholding Lord Buddha,
How could any ordinary perception remain?
Even on the tenth ground, he never experienced confusion. ^14-24

Having seen that very truth, I pay homage.
Abandon the physical conduct of turning one’s back.
At one time, holding a cane to his heart, he said:
This hearing-lineage Dharma, a wish-fulfilling jewel-
Do not let it leak to those without fortune. ^14-25

In former times, when the precious Master lived,
Except for a person such as myself,
It was not granted to any others.
This is not a matter of being stingy with the Dharma,
It is intended for those who possess the proper spiritual disposition and receptivity. ^14-26

Not writing down the oral lineage in letters,
Nor revealing the symbolic lineage merely in words,
Nor singing one’s meditative experiences as songs in the marketplace,
Nor engaging in the eccentric ways of a mad ascetic, ^14-27

But, following exactly the words of the Buddha, the Lord, and the Guru,
Having put the unmistaken meaning into practice,
And according to the differing capacities of the discipleswho are worthy vessels,
May the primordial wisdom of the Dharma-kaya be directly realized. ^14-28

May the primordial wisdom of the Dharma-kaya be directly realized.
Put on these new robes and remain here.
You are the holder of the teachings ^14-29

Later, because there would be a need for a guide
who would serve as the crown-jewel of many great and venerable ones,
He said, “With the necessary conditions complete,
Establish yourself in the full state and discipline of a bhikshu (fully ordained monk). ^14-30

Just as I was urged by the command of my revered Dharma father,
I went before the great abbot Shamarapa
And the master Marpa Chomtang Drung,
And fully adopted the discipline of pure conduct. ^14-31

Then the Precious Master spoke these words:
My son, you shall have assemblies as numerous as these,
And among them will be many supreme disciples. Equal in realization and abandonment.
You are prophesied to attain buddhahood in this very realm. ^14-32

In that secluded mountain hermitage, free from distraction,
With no attachment, and for the benefit of wandering beings,
I practiced the profound instructions on supplication.
By practicing, I repaid the kindness of the six classes of mother beings. ^14-33

At the cliffs of Götsang, I sealed myself in retreat.
The continuous journey of outer distraction of activities –
And the inner rope of dualistic grasping at apprehended objects and an apprehending subject are completely severed.
Within the mind of clear light, there is no distinction between meditative equipoise and post-meditation. ^14-34

From the glorious, great Tilopa
Down to me, the young monk Trakpal,
There has been only a lineage of sugatas—
Not a single ordinary being has interrupted it. ^14-35

He was acknowledged as Dipamkara, the Buddha of former times;
As the protector Maitreya, The victorious Buddha of the future;
And as Shakyamuni, the crown of sages, The present guide of beings.
Thus, he was recognized. As the Victor of the three times. ^14-36

Nevertheless, as a skillful means for taming disciples,
From the moment he entered the monastic life,
He never touched meat or alcohol,
And no woman ever entered his private chamber. ^14-37

From eminent and learned Geshes
Down to powerless, blind beggars,
At the offering feasts held in his residence, From the household of the patron, Ké-saNaktsang.
He gave everyone an equal share of food and drink— barley flour and tea. ^14-38

Outwardly, the four elements, planets, stars, and seasons;
Inwardly, the channels, syllables, winds, mind, and chakras;
And secretly, spontaneous awareness, And direct encounters within his awakened mind.
All arose as the wheel of Dharma- Through the purity of dependent origination. ^14-39

Precious Lord, glory of samsara and nirvana,
You are inseparable from the fatherly protector of beings.
Those fortunate disciples with pure karma
Perceived him in many ways-Through the purity of their own perception: ^14-40

Some saw him as the Buddhas of the five families;
Some saw him as Great Compassion, Avalokiteshvara;
Some saw him as the lord of the teachings;
Some saw him as the mandala of Cakrasamvara; ^14-41

Many saw him as an exalted sugata of former times;
And some saw his single form appearing as many.
Some saw the two Precious Masters sitting face to face
And engaging in profound Dharma discussion. ^14-42

All these were miraculous manifestations of the Precious Master;
And the Precious Master himself- Was a miraculous manifestation of mind.
Therefore, in the ultimate sense, the guru is one’s own mind. ^14-43

In general, from the time I met the Precious Lord,
I practiced whatever he taught- And whatever pleased him.
Whenever I saw his form,I welcomed him with a joyful smile;
Whenever I heard his speech, I listened with faith and devotion. ^14-44

Regarding all his deeds and liberated conduct,
I developed the firm conviction- That he was truly a nirmanakaya. ^14-45

Although the beings to be tamed- Throughout samsara and nirvana are countless,
Within the perception shared by us, Great assemblies gathered around him.
Without distinction between master and disciples,
many spiritual heirs arose, equal in abandonment and realization ^14-46

In China, the great Khoyo
Cared for an assembly of seven hundred disciples.
In the land of China, Sejé- Gathered five hundred disciples.
In Jang, AgöZhikpo gathered ten thousand; ^14-47

The great Lord Dzagön gathered three hundred thousand;
The Repa of Göshö gathered two thousand;
PuwoJamsé gathered three hundred;
And Gharang of Minyak gathered five hundred. ^14-48

In Latö, Dranak Senggé,
And in Dokham, Rechen and Bomgön—
Each cared for an assembly- Of three hundred disciples.
At SumbuSertshang in Minyak,
Countless realized disciples assembled around him. ^14-49

Muto, Gatön, and Lungkhapa
Promised never to separate from the Lord himself.
Shang Osha and Tsilungpa of Ön
Brought many assembled disciples to spiritual maturity. ^14-50

NyemoDzégom of northern Zaram
Miraculously presented offerings every day.
In Kashmir, Mayo Gomnak
Became the abbot of the glorious Bodhgaya. ^14-51

At Rutok, KongpoGomnak
Distributed bowls of saffron at Taklung.
In Nyal, there was one calledSangye Rongtön,
Whose mind became one with the master’s wisdom- mind Through the transmission of Dharma’s meaning. ^14-52

In Simhala was the great meditator Lhukgé;
At the sacred place of Jalandhara was Manipa;
In Uddiyana was Drakpa Gyaltsen;
And in Asura was Lama Garpa.
These and others were the eighteen great heart-sons.
Each of them gathered disciples beyond all counting. ^14-53

What has been told here- Is only the briefest summary.
In general, all who saw, heard, remembered, or touched him,
And every being to be tamed-Throughout the realms of ssamsara and nirvana,
Were ripened by him- For great and perfect awakening. ^14-54

When the Lord intended to turn his mind toward another pure realm,
To his devoted disciples and patrons,
The Lord, the Dharma King, spoke these words:
My children, keep these words within your hearts. ^14-55

All of you who supplicate with devotion
And render material service,
Will attain fulfillment through your connection with me.
Because I supplicated the Precious Lord, all the excellent qualities
Of samsara and nirvana arose.
You too should supplicate him-With all the strength you possess. ^14-56

All those who place their hope and trust in me
I shall guide,For I am inseparable from Lord Ratna.
With these prophetic words, He brought them relief, encouragement, and inspiration
And all attained the accomplishments- Of meditative experience and realization. ^14-57

The Lord attained the vajra body,
Although for him there is neither coming nor going, neither departure nor remaining,
In the supreme eastern pure realm of Manifest Joy,
Having pledged to be the protector, King Akshobhya,
There, surrounded by countless disciples,
He continuously turns the wheel of the Mahayana Dharma. ^14-58

O Protector, your compassionate manifestations are limitless.
Until the very end of all future time,
The undisputed emanations of the Lord himself
May appear successively among his spiritual heirs. ^14-59

Precious Lord, you bear great responsibilities and accomplish great aims;
Precious one, you bear small responsibilities and accomplish small aims;
Precious one, every connection with you becomes meaningful;
Precious one, glorious protector of both samsara and nirvana; ^14-60

For me, who supplicates you, and for all our aged mother beings, as limitless as space,
O Father, arouse the mighty power of your immeasurable compassion.
Hold us always in your unfailing love, without ever being separated from us,
And guide us along the path of the supreme vehicle of Secret Mantra.
May our minds merge inseparably with your awakened mind.
May we then become guides for the mother beings of the six realms. ^14-61

The precious wish-fulfilling jewel of Dharma -Is the mind of awakening, bodhicitta.
Whoever supplicates it will obtain everything they need and desire.
Through hundreds of thousands- Of auspicious blessings arising from the power of your compassion,
May the whole expanse of samsara and nirvana be filled with goodness and virtue. ^14-62

May this precious teaching of the Dharma practice lineage
Pervade the ocean of infinite realms.
May all karmic actions and afflictions that arise from ignorance be dispelled,
And may the light of primordial wisdom remain forever. ^14-63

<small>This comes from the biographies of the Golden Rosary of those residing at the Great Seat of Glorious Riwoche.</small> ^14-64

## 15. Condensed Birth Stories of Thangpa ^15-0

The victorious Vajradhara in the Aknishtha realm,
Indrabhuti in the practice place,
The glorious Tashi Pel in this land of snow,
The well-known Thangpa Chenpo, to you I pay homage! ^15-1

The lord Akshobhya in the realm free from dust (viraja),
The lord Avalokiteshvara in Potala,
The incomparable Tashi Pel in this land of snow,
The well-known Thangpa Chenpo, to you I pay homage! ^15-2

Rasa Trulnang, the King of Emanation,
At U, the supreme Arya Chayulwa in the upper part,
The incomparable Tashi Pel in this plain of Talkung,
The well-known Thangpa Chenpo, to you I pay homage! ^15-3

<small>This Supplication of Condensed Birth stories of Taklungpa Chenpo is written by Bhikshu Namgyal Rinchen Pal Zangpo.</small> ^15-4

## 16. Praise of the Previous Lives of TaklungThangpa Rinpoche ^16-0

Although your enlightened mind is completely pure and inseparable from the Dharmadhatu,
Through the various miraculous manifestations of great primordial wisdom,
You bring to spiritual maturity sentient beings as limitless as space.
I supplicate the glorious TaklungThangpa Rinpoche. ^16-1

In the eastern pure Buddha-field, free from all defilements,
To countless assemblies of bodhisattvas,
You continuously turn the wheel of the Mahayana Dharma.
I praise you, the victorious protector Akshobhya Buddha. ^16-2

Gathering together the compassion of all the bodhisattvas,
You manifested as a spiritual guide in this Saha world.
Your power, deeds, and enlightened activities are beyond imagination.
I praise you, the protector Avalokiteshvara. ^16-3

From the sacred treasury of Dharma in the land of Uddiyana,
You preserved the secret teachings of the Teacher’s enlightened body, speech, and mind.
You compiled the teachings and became a wheel- turning ruler among holders of knowledge-mantras.
I bow to Indrabhuti, the embodiment of primordial wisdom. ^16-4

Among the ocean of shravaka disciples who attended upon the Buddha,
You were supreme in retaining the teachings you had heard.
You compiled the vast collections of countless sutras.
I bow to the noble Ananda, renowned for his extensive learning. ^16-5

You were the supreme regent of the unequalled Teacher’s doctrine.
You served the great elders with devotion.
With the three kinds of nectar, you relieved the burning suffering caused by the three obscurations.
I bow to the noble lay devotee Dharmatala. ^16-6

In this snowy land of Tibet, you established the ten virtuous actions,
Making their royal laws like a golden yoke of discipline.
You opened the way for the sacred Buddhist teachings to flourish.
I pay homage to you, who appeared as King SongtsenGampo. ^16-7

Through the profound view and the vast conduct,
And through the blessed oral instructions of the lineage of practice,
You brightly illuminated the land of Tibet like a shining lamp.
I bow to DromtönGyalweJungne. ^16-8

Through serving your lama in the three ways that please him,
The auspicious circumstances for your enlightened activities naturally came together.
Your knowledge, realization, and compassion were beyond imagination.
I bow to the spiritual friend, the master Chayulpa. ^16-9

Outwardly, you maintained the training of individual liberation.
Inwardly, you practiced the generation and completion stages of the Vajrayana.
You became the life-supporting pillar of the teachings of their essential meaning.
I respectfully bow to the protector Mangalashri. ^16-10

From now until I attain unsurpassed enlightenment,
May I follow the liberating life and example of the incomparable Jetsun Taklung Thangpa.
May I train in accordance with his life of liberation,
And may I accomplish his three vajra secrets of enlightened body, speech, and mind. ^16-11

<small>This was written by the Buddhist monk Ngawang Tashi Paldrub.</small> ^16-12

## 17. A Supplication Prayer Recounting the Liberating Life of Sangye WönDrakpa Pal Özer Zangpo, the Emanation of the Supreme Venerable Master ^17-0

Homage to the precious Guru Hevajra! ^17-1

O incomparable protector, you descended from the expanse of space.
Your spiritual lineage is the unchanging lineage of Vajradhara.
The dakinis gave prophecies and urged the two, Jo and Shak.
I supplicate you, the one endowed with boundless compassion. ^17-2

Although you entered your mother’s womb, you were free from worldly attachment and aversion.
You intentionally took birth, thinking only of benefiting sentient beings.
You practiced the six daily sessions of Hevajra yoga without negligence.
I supplicate to you, who had trained in the two stages of tantric practice. ^17-3

You were born peacefully, without causing suffering to your mother.
When your umbilical cord was cut, the sound changed into the music of a lute.
You spoke twenty-one verses of the sacred Dharma.
I supplicate you, who resembled the son of King Shuddhodana, the Buddha. ^17-4

Surrounded by rainbow light, you rose into the sky and remained there.
You recited the Añja mantra and the Sanskrit vowels and consonants.
You said to your mother, “Keep this extremely secret.”
I supplicate to you, who performed your activities in a concealed manner. ^17-5

When you were invited to the center of a pure and sacred place,
You received the prophecy of a hero and saw the wings of Hevajra’sgaruda.
At the age of three, the treasury of vajra songs burst forth from you.
I supplicate you, the exceptionally noble one. ^17-6

Like a miraculous child, you performed many playful activities.
You explained many kinds of letters, sounds, and systems of language.
You demonstrated various ways of making images of the victorious Buddhas.
I supplicate you, who perfected all fields of learning. ^17-7

You shaped the figure of a naga from solid rock as easily as molding butter.
Upon water, you established a firm and lasting sacred image.
You entered the unfathomable depths of the earth as swiftly as a thunderbolt.
I supplicate you, the one without equal. ^17-8

You caused downward-flowing water to rise upward like flames.
You walked steadily across steep cliffs as though they were ordinary paths.
You showed especially great compassion toward helpless children.
I supplicate you, the supreme son of the victorious Buddhas. ^17-9

A piece of wood, only a finger’s length, blazed by itself during the night.
You raised a blessed mandala into the expanse of the sky.
You transformed the sacred substance of mada into uncontaminated nectar.
I supplicate you, who performed wonders resembling magical illusions. ^17-10

In a single night, you visited the great sacred places of Uddiyana.
You received complete empowerments from the eight renowned mahasiddhas.
You were given a fresh, moist ear of victorious rice.
I supplicate you, who possessed extraordinary spiritual accomplishments. ^17-11

In the morning, you demonstrated how to guide gatherings of kings and ministers.
In the evening, you appeared as an ordained monk and turned the wheel of Dharma.
You gave nectar from your mouth that tasted like sweet sugar.
I supplicate you, who performed many activities for the benefit of sentient beings. ^17-12

You brought forth a supreme image of Hayagriva from the side of a rock.
Through the naga-tree Brahmin, you subdued a yaksha by your power.
The life-essence of NyenkharNakpo was offered directly to you.
I supplicate you, the one endowed with miraculous power and strength. ^17-13

You opened the doors of hidden treasures at several great sacred places of accomplished masters.
In the middle of a fireplace, you revealed the nine deities of Hevajra.
Through skillful use of auspicious dependent connections, you turned away harm threatening the region.
I supplicate you, whose blessings reached perfection. ^17-14

You directly inspired the sorrowful songs of the wisdom dakinis.
You explained the meanings of questions and answers in symbolic language, in accordance with the tantras.
An ocean of dakinis honored you as the ornament upon their crowns.
I supplicate you, who mastered the meaning of symbolic language. ^17-15

You appeared as the sacred regent of the Buddha, the Teacher.
The glory of your meaningful and excellent name spread widely.
Inseparable as father and son, you radiated miraculous rays of light.
I supplicate you, who possessed the excellent marks and signs of an enlightened being. ^17-16

You remained free from the faults of the two hundred and fifty monastic precepts.
You mastered bodhicitta mind-training and the practice of exchanging yourself with others.
You directly realized the profound generation and completion stages.
I supplicate you, who perfected the three sets of vows. ^17-17

From Buddhas down to shravakas and pratyekabuddhas,
You revealed many different visions to those who had the appropriate karmic connections.
You developed deep and unshakable certainty, free from dispute.
I supplicate you, whose every teaching heard was meaningful. ^17-18

You practiced Naropa’s path of skillful means, applying the essential points of channels and winds.
You brought the moving vital winds into the central channel, the dhuti.
You perceived everything that arose as the great true nature of reality.
I supplicate you, whose mind realized emptiness. ^17-19

You beheld the mandalas of the ocean of all victorious Buddhas.
You understood the realizations of all the learned and accomplished masters of India and Tibet.
You received prophecies from all the dakinis of the three places.
I supplicate you, whose activities for the benefit of beings were spontaneously accomplished. ^17-20

To those who were deeply confused, you especially demonstrated powerful yogic conduct.
You twisted the handle of a white stone as though it were woolen thread.
You caused great thunderbolts to fall like rain from a cloudless sky.
I supplicate you, who subdued wrong views. ^17-21

Upon the waters of the autumn month, you arranged a silken pathway for the dakinis.
At that time, you prepared to depart for another pure realm of emanation.
When supplications were offered, you once again looked upon your disciples with compassion.
I supplicate you, who continued to care for fortunate disciples. ^17-22

You left raised footprints upon the solid rocks at Thaden.
You spread the sacred Dharma extensively to beings without distinction of direction.
You guided the three kinds of spiritual practitioners onto the path of enlightenment.
I supplicate you, whose mind was filled with bodhicitta. ^17-23

You transformed this remote and uncivilized region into a pure Buddha-field.
You clearly manifested the arrangement of Vajrasana, the Vajra Seat.
You opened the treasure doors of all desirable enjoyments and necessities.
I supplicate you, who resembled a universal wheel- turning monarch. ^17-24

Jñānatapa escorted the sacred Dharma to your door.
Upari offered a treasury of scriptures into your hands.
You received the complete oral transmissions of all the Buddha’s teachings.
I supplicate you, whose aspiration prayers possessed great power. ^17-25

You constructed many supreme sacred supports representing enlightened body, speech, and mind.
You spread the teachings of both Sutra and Tantra extensively.
You brought the traditions of teaching and practice to complete fulfillment.
I supplicate you, the heir and representative of the victorious Buddhas. ^17-26

Through the power of miraculous abilities, you traveled throughout the three-thousandfold universe.
You entrusted the blessed lineage transmission to one who was like yourself.
You promised to appear again as an emanation to uphold the Dharma lineage.
I supplicate you, who worked for the benefit of future generations. ^17-27

When the time came for you to depart for the pure realm, you adorned your body with six ornaments.
In your hands, you held sacred substances symbolizing skillful means and wisdom.
You rose gracefully and transferred your consciousness into the great unobstructed expanse.
I supplicate you, the incomparable one. ^17-28

At that time, the earth shook in six different ways.
Many kinds of musical sounds were heard throughout the ten directions.
Hevajra yogic forms were seen dissolving into the sky.
I supplicate you, who attained the supreme great accomplishment. ^17-29

When your sacred body was placed upon the funeral fire, it was difficult to ignite.
When Punyashri offered supplications,
Fire arose from the center of your heart, and the body was consumed in an instant.
I supplicate you, who showed exceptional compassion toward me. ^17-30

Five-colored rainbows gathered in the sky like clouds.
Many different flowers fell, filling the atmosphere.
Various rays of light radiated throughout the ten directions.
I supplicate you, the one whose miraculous wonders were extraordinary. ^17-31

As sacred supports of your enlightened mind, the pañcarekha appeared,
Together with a clearly visible syllable HAM, symbolizing the unborn nature.
Countless relics, sacred remains, and small reliquary stupas appeared.
I supplicate you, whose signs of spiritual accomplishment were exceptional. ^17-32

You realized the meaning of the syllable AH, which represents the unborn nature.
You emanated from the enlightened mind of the primordial Buddha, the boundless ancestor of all Buddhas.
You were the spiritual son who carried out the enlightened activities of all Buddhas of the ten directions.
I supplicate you, whose excellent name was truly meaningful. ^17-33

In this five-hundred-year period, you were as rare as the udumbara flower.
You were supreme among those who worked for the teachings of Buddha Shakyamuni.
You were a spiritual son directly emanated from Avalokiteshvara.
I supplicate you, the emanation of the Buddha. ^17-34

The great sound of your Dharma teachings spread throughout the ten directions.
Like a glorious wish-fulfilling jewel, you fulfilled the hopes and wishes of living beings.
You radiated the light of your blessings upon all those endowed with faith.
I supplicate you, whose enlightened activities were excellent. ^17-35

The confusion of ignorance was purified within the expanse of the true nature of reality.
Through the power of aspiration prayers, the qualities of nirvana fully blossomed.
You were the lineage-holding spiritual heir of all the lamas of the three times.
I supplicate you, whose supreme name was renowned everywhere. ^17-36

Your supreme name possessed four qualities, representing the four kinds of enlightened activities.
You dwelt in the supreme pure Buddha-field, free from all defilements.
You declared that your enlightened activities had been completed in the realm of Mirror-like Arrangement.
I supplicate you, who knew the past, present, and future. ^17-37

The wish-fulfilling jewel beautifully adorns the crown of the lord of the waters.
A city of bliss stands upon the summit of Mount Meru.
Through the glory of merit, I have witnessed the excellent qualities of my lama.
O kind and compassionate master, I can never forget your kindness. ^17-38

Just as a bee gathers the sweet essence from a garden of lotus flowers,
And just as a swan separates milk from the waters of the ocean,
Through my faith, I have received the spiritual accomplishments and blessings of my lama.
O kind and compassionate master, I can never forget your kindness. ^17-39

Although my lama has departed to the pure realm free from defilements,
Your compassionate vision knows neither distance nor nearness.
Please quickly catch me with the iron hook of bodhicitta.
Please draw me toward you with the lasso of the wisdom of emptiness. ^17-40

Please rescue me from the deep and dangerous ravine of self-grasping.
Please wash away the stains of faults, downfalls, and defilements.
Please release me from the iron chains of the eight worldly concerns and samsara.
Please remove the chronic disease of attachment to one-sided views and disturbing emotions. ^17-41

Please guide me onto the path of liberation through pure ethical discipline.
Please show me the ultimate view of the profound path.
Please cause the tender sprout of bodhicitta, the seed of Buddhahood, to grow within me.
Please enable me to attain the final result of omniscient Buddhahood. ^17-42

<small>This was composed by Skuzhang Lha Sonam Pal.</small> ^17-43

## 18. Increasing the Excellency of Samsara and Nirvana: Offering Clouds of the Sixteen Elders of the Buddha. ^18-0

Oneself and others, all sentient beings equal to the reaches of space in the three realms, we all take refuge to the the most reverend, the root and lineage holders, the glorious gurus! ^18-1

To all the bhavat buddhas, we take refuge! ^18-2

To all the sacred dharmas, I take refuge! ^18-3

To all the noble sanghas, I take refuge! ^18-4

In the buddha, the dharma, and the supreme sangha assembly – ^18-5

Util I attain enlightenment, I take refuge! ^18-6

Through the merit of engaging in generosity and so forth by me, ^18-7

For the benefit of all beings, may I attain enlightenment! <small>(Three times)</small> ^18-8

All sentient beings, may they enjoy happiness and the causes of happiness! ^18-9

May they be free from suffering and the causes of suffering! ^18-10

May they be free from suffering and never be separated from happiness! ^18-11

May they be directly seen the equanimity, the nature (dharmata) of all phenomena free from the three afflictions: attachment, aversion, and bewilderment! ^18-12

May all beings have happiness
May they be free from suffering
Rejoicing that they always remain in happiness
And practice the sameness, the equanimity! ^18-13

<small>Having recited the refuge, cultivation of enlightened mind (bodhicitta), and the four immeasurable thoughts,</small> ^18-14

Blessings of the Three Jewels ^18-15

The great power of being perfectly completed the two accumulations ^18-16

The complete purity of true expanse (dharmadhatu) and inconceivable truth ^18-17

The power of complete purification of buddha- fields, such as the practice of the pure fields of the bodhisattvas, and so on ^18-18

Through the power of our conviction, in this very place, in the center, endowing the adornment, array, and all the excellent qualities of all buddha fields, an immeasurable celestial mansion with four sides and four gates made from the seven types of precious substances, ^18-19

The joy of dharma flavour (dharmarasa) and stability of the great bliss (by the way of great bliss) ^18-20

The array of abode of tathagata, arranged by the ocean of offering clouds arisen from the emanation of the noble samantabhadra, in the essence of which is the variegated lotus with sixteen petals ^18-21

That in turn, produced a rim at the navel. ^18-22

<small>With holding incense and various flowers, by playing the cymbals (rol-mo), the invitation is:</small> ^18-23

The blessings of the ocean of jewels and the two accumulations
Amassed with directly examined, the true expanse (dharmadhatu)
Through inconceivable great truth of total purity
The entire arranged ornaments of the fields of the ten directions ^18-24

The beauty of which is altogether established here in this place,
The Great Sage (mahamuni) is the lord of the unrivalled teachings
Along with the retinues of assembly of the noble elders (sthavira)
Please come and sit through the power of spiritual commitment! ^18-25

Through your compassion, the beings or trainees in this age of strife (kaliyugam)
Leading them on the path of complete liberation (vimukti-marga), you are the protector of beings
The supreme Buddha (Muni) and the great Elders (sthavira)
Together with the retinues, please come to this very place! ^18-26

The protector of beings, the lion of the Shakyas (Shakyasimha),
Have placed the teachings of the Buddha in his hand
Opening the chest of precious teachings and discourses
The great arhat Elders, please be seated! ^18-27

Since you are invited to spread the sacred dharma,
For the benefit of beings, I make offerings, please come!
As the Buddha had commended to hold the victory banner of the teachings:
SthaviraAngita or Angaja Ajita, and the one who lives in the forest (vanavasin) ^18-28

Kalika, Vajriputra, and Bhadra,
Kanakavatsa, Bharadvaja, and Kañcanavati
Arya Bakula and Rahula,
Chudapanthaka, Bharadvaja, and Pindola, ^18-29

Margakhyata, Nagasena, Gopaka (gopayati), and Abhedya,
For the benefit of beings, I make offerings, please come!
The excellent abandonment and pristine wisdom, even the Buddha,
Due to the beings to be tamed, appears in the form of a Hearer (Shravaka), ^18-30

Protecting the dharma of exceptional enlightened activities –
The sixteen Elders, please come here and be seated!
Requesting lay practitioners (upasaka) to protect and listening to the true words,
I pay homage to all the retinues of the Three Jewels present here! ^18-31

Inviting you all in the island of precious merit,
For the benefit of beings, I make offerings, please come! ^18-32

<small>Play cymbals (sil-nyen). They have arrived from their abodes as invited through the power of prayers and aspirations and sat down on their cushion.</small> ^18-33

In the center, on a lion throne, lotus, and moon (disk), ^18-34

The Bhagavat, Tathagata Arhat, completely perfect Buddha (samyaksambuddha) Shakyamuni, the color of his body is likened to that of a refined gold, His right hand is in earth-touching mudra and left hand is in the mudra of equanimity, ^18-35

His legs are in vajra posture, ^18-36

Adorned by the thirty-two major noble marks ^18-37

And eighty minor marks, ^18-38

Donning the three types of saffron dharma robes The light of which illuminates throughout all world realms. ^18-39

The four petals in the east resides the noble Mahāsthvira Aṅgaja, ^18-40

Holding an incense pot in his folded palms and a whisk (cāmaram) in his left armpit, ^18-41

The two hands of Sthavira Ajita are in the mudrā of equanimity ^18-42

The right hand of SthaviraVanavāsin is in a peaceful mudrā of the threatening gesture (tarjanī) and the left hand is holding a whisk ^18-43

SthaviraKālika holds earrings in his two hands, ^18-44

On the four petals in the South – ^18-45

SthaviraVajrīputra with his right hand in peaceful mudrā of the threatening gesture (tarjanī) and left hand holds a whisk, ^18-46

Sthavira Bhadra’s right hand is in the mudrā of expounding the dharma and left hand is in the mudrā of equanimity, ^18-47

SthaviraKanakavatsa holds a jewelled lasso in his two hands, ^18-48

The two hands of Sthavira Bharadvaja and Kanchanavati are in the mudrā of equanimity. ^18-49

On the four petals in the West – ^18-50

In Sthavira Bakula’s hand holds a mongoose, ^18-51

Sthavira Rahul holds a jewelled crown in his two hands, ^18-52

The two hands of SthaviraChudapanthaka are in the mudrā of equanimity, ^18-53

Sthavira Bharadvaja Pindola holds a scripture in his right hand and alms bowl in his left hand. ^18-54

On the four petals in the North – ^18-55

SthaviraPanthaka holds a scripture in his right hand and his left hand is in the mudrā of teaching the dharma, ^18-56

Sthavira Nāgasena holds a vase in his right hand and a staff in his left hand, ^18-57

Sthavira Gopaka holds scriptures in his two hands, ^18-58

SthaviraAbhedya holds a precious bodhi stupa in between his folded palms, ^18-59

All of them donning the three types of yellow dharma robes, they are peaceful, disciplined, and having many arhat (worthy ones) retinues. ^18-60

Inside the Eastern gate, the noble Mahā-UpāsakaDharmatala holds a whisk in his right hand and a vase in his left hand, ^18-61

He has a topknot of matted hair, ^18-62

Donning silken robes that gently flutter ^18-63

Carrying numerous scriptural collections (piṭaka) ^18-64

The Amitābha Buddha residing in the sky before oneself, ^18-65

Emanated assembly of tigers on his right knee, ^18-66

Together with the assembly of Upāsakas. ^18-67

At the four gates, the White Dhṛtarāṣṭra (guardian king of the east) holding a lute (vīṇa), ^18-68

The Blue Virūḍhaka (guardian king of the south) holding a sword, ^18-69

The Red Dreadful Eye, Virūpākṣa (guardian king of the west) holding a stupa and snake lasso, ^18-70

The Yellow Vaiśravaṇa (guardian king of the north) holding a victory banner and mongoose, ^18-71

All four of them are adorned with the five types of armors and silken garments, and together with assembly of retinues, they clearly appear to completely guard the teachings. ^18-72

<small>With this perform consecration of offerings, two water (offerings), cymbals, the seven precious royal emblems, the five desirable sense objects, the auspicious substances and signs, maṇḍalam, and seven branch prayer. To consecrate the offerings by reciting the Recollection of the Three Jewels, eight verses of “when you were born, chief of bipeds (humans),” and so on, is:</small> ^18-73

OṃAḥHūṃ <small>(Three times)</small> ^18-74

The truth of the Three Jewels, ^18-75

All the buddhas and bodhisattvas, ^18-76

The nature of various precious grounds equal to that of the palms of one’s hand, blessed by the great noble Elders (sthaviraḥ), ^18-77

The pure, delightful, and pleasant dwelling place of noble ones ^18-78

Free from all types of fearsomeness, ^18-79

Powerful and free, ^18-80

Beautified by wish-fulfilling tree and various precious pleasure groves, ^18-81

Beautiful grassy meadows ^18-82

Beautiful and variegated flowers ^18-83

From the sweet chirping sound of various birds, endowed with the sound of sacred dharma ^18-84

Bathing pond endowed with the eight qualities of water, ^18-85

Adorned by waterfalls, ponds, ^18-86

Spring waters, and so on – all necessities, on the ground with various fragrances, celestial mansion made from various precious substances as causes, ^18-87

The vast and spacious, bright and unhindered pleasure gardens of sanghas, ^18-88

Adorned with infinite ornamentations of divine substances, ^18-89

Endowed with the three gateways of liberation and amidst the blaze of great light rays, at the navel of sixteen petalled lotus, if one touches the precious throne and seat of lotus and moon with pure and excellent, vast and rgya ‘bolba, infinity on the seat of bliss, the offering clouds are pure and attractive, arisen from the biography of Samantabhadra, may it become possessed with inconceivable offering clouds! ^18-90

<small>Consecrate this three times.</small> ^18-91

In the infinite oceans galaxies,
As much offering waters as there is,
I have arranged this with respect,
All that is, to the victorious ones together with their offspring, ^18-92

I offer that much to all,
You, who are endowed with compassion,
Accept this as you please
And please work for the welfare of sentient beings! ^18-93

<small>Also, with these two lines,</small> apply bathing water, flower, incense, lamp, fragrant water, food, cymbal, <small>and so on in bed, perform two water offerings, together with playing the drum and cymbal.</small> ^18-94

The lord of humans, these seven precious emblems,
The buddhas and their offspring, without exception,
I arrange and make imagined offerings to them
May this serve as treasury for the infinite beings! ^18-95

<small>With this, offer the seven precious emblems</small> ^18-96

Having the form, sound, smell, taste, and touch,
The most supreme resources and possessions
I offer them by faith
Please accept them as you please! ^18-97

<small>With this, offer the five desirable sense objects.</small> ^18-98

Just as the lord of the gods (devendraḥ) have won the battle from the demigods (asuraḥ)
And the eight auspicious goddesses made offerings,
Likewise, to the victorious ones and bodhisattvas
I make this offering of the eight auspicious substances! ^18-99

<small>With this, offer the eight auspicious substances.</small> ^18-100

The worldly householders relying on the glory,
The bilva, targa together with an excellent vase,
Just as it is revered and make auspiciousness,
I make this offerings to the victorious ones together with their offspring, ^18-101

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ)
And the sixteen noble Elders,
The auspicious calf and curl of svastika
I offer them with respect and devotion! ^18-102

To the Bhagavat, lord of Sages (munīndraḥ)
And the sixteen noble Elders,
A golden wheel with rims
I offer it with respect and devotion! ^18-103

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
A white parasol with golden handle,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-104

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
The victorious banner with blazing crest of jewels,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-105

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
The excellent vase that fulfills all wishes,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-106

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
A white lotus with thousand petals,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-107

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
A pleasant conch-shell curling to the right,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-108

To the Bhagavat, the lord of Sages (munīndraḥ),
And the sixteen noble Elders,
A golden fish painted robe,
I offer it with respect and devotion. ^18-109

Oṃaṣṭamaṅgalapūjāmeghasamudraspharaṇasamayehūṃ | ^18-110

<small>With this, offer the eight auspicious signs with respect to the application of maṇḍala offering.</small> ^18-111

<small>|Recite the hundred syllable mantra before.</small> ^18-112

oṃvajrabhūmiāḥhūṃ ^18-113

The earth is the golden ground, completely pure, full of beauty and power. ^18-114

oṃvajrarekheāḥhūṃ ^18-115

The circular iron mountain wall completely surrounds it; in the centre is the letter hūṃ— ^18-116

Meru, the king of mountains; ^18-117

In the East is Pūrvavideha, in the South is Jambudvīpa, ^18-118

In the West is Aparagodānīya, in the North is Uttarakuru. ^18-119

Beside them are Deha and Videha, Cāmara and Aparacāmara, ^18-120

Śāthā and Uttaramantriṇa, ^18-121

Kurava and Kaurava. ^18-122

The jewel mountain, the wish-fulfilling tree, ^18-123

The wish-fulfilling cow, the harvest which needs no sowing, ^18-124

The precious wheel, the precious gem, ^18-125

The precious queen, the precious minister, ^18-126

The precious elephant, the precious horse, ^18-127

The precious general, the vase of great treasure, ^18-128

The goddess of beauty, the goddess of garlands, ^18-129

The goddess of song, the goddess of dance, ^18-130

The goddess of flowers, the goddess of incense, ^18-131

The goddess of light, the goddess of perfume, ^18-132

The sun, the moon, the precious umbrella, ^18-133

The royal banner victorious in all directions: ^18-134

There is all the bountiful wealth of gods and men, complete and flawless—All this: I offer— ^18-135

Please accept it, with compassion, for the benefit of beings. ^18-136

Having accepted it, please grant your blessing! ^18-137

<small>Offer the extensive maṇḍala offering.</small> ^18-138

### The seven-branch prayer is: ^18-1-0

<small>(1) Prostration</small> ^18-139

To all the buddhas, the lions of the human race,
In all directions of the universe, through past and present and future:
To every single one of you, I bow in homage;
Devotion fills my body, speech and mind. ^18-140

Through the power of this prayer, aspiring to Good Action,
All the victorious ones appear, vivid here before my mind
And I multiply my body as many times as atoms in the universe,
Each one bowing in prostration to all the buddhas. ^18-141

<small>2. Offering</small> ^18-142

In every atom preside as many buddhas as there are atoms,
And around them, all their bodhisattva heirs:
And so I imagine them filling
Completely the entire space of reality. ^18-143

Saluting them with an endless ocean of praise,
With the sounds of an ocean of different melodies
I sing of the buddhas’ noble qualities,
And praise all those who have gone to perfect bliss. ^18-144

To every buddha, I make offerings:
Of the loveliest flowers, of beautiful garlands,
Of music and perfumed ointments, the best of parasols,
The brightest lamps and finest incense. ^18-145

To every buddha, I make offerings:
Exquisite garments and the most fragrant scents,
Powdered incense, heaped as high as Mount Meru,
Arranged in perfect symmetry. ^18-146

Then the vast and unsurpassable offerings—
Inspired by my devotion to all the buddhas, and
Moved by the power of my faith in Good Actions—
I prostrate and offer to all you victorious ones. ^18-147

<small>(3) Confession of Non-Virtuous Deeds</small> ^18-148

Whatever negative acts I have committed,
While driven by desire, hatred and ignorance,
With my body, my speech and also with my mind,
Before you, I confess and purify each and every one. ^18-149

<small>(4) Rejoicing Other’s Good Deeds</small> ^18-150

With a heart full of delight, I rejoice at all the merits
Of buddhas and bodhisattvas,
Pratyekabuddhas, those in training and the arhats beyond training,
And every living being, throughout the entire universe. ^18-151

<small>(5) Imploring the Buddhas to Turn the Wheel of Dharma</small> ^18-152

You who are like beacons of light shining through the worlds,
Who passed through the stages of enlightenment, to attain buddhahood, freedom from all attachment,
I exhort you: all of you protectors,
Turn the unsurpassable wheel of Dharma. ^18-153

<small>(6) Requesting the Buddhas Not to Enter Nirvāṇa</small> ^18-154

Joining my palms together, I pray
To you who intend to pass into nirvāṇa,
Remain, for aeons as many as the atoms in this world,
And bring well-being and happiness to all living beings. ^18-155

<small>(7) Dedication of Merit</small> ^18-156

What little virtue I have gathered through my homage,
Through offering, confession, and rejoicing,
Through exhortation and prayer—all of it
I dedicate to the enlightenment of all beings! ^18-157

### Remembering the Jewels ^18-2-0

I prostrate to the Three Jewels! ^18-158

Thus, the Buddha, the accomplished transcendent conqueror, the tathagata who has attained thatness, the arhat who has conquered all foes, is a perfectly and completely enlightened buddha, ^18-159

endowed with awareness and worthy of reverence, ^18-160

the Sugata who has gone to bliss, ^18-161

knower of the entire world, ^18-162

guide and tamer of beings, ^18-163

unsurpassable, ^18-164

teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, accomplished transcendent conqueror, ^18-165

that Tathagata is compatible with the causes of pure merit, ^18-166

he does not waste the sources of virtue, ^18-167

he is supremely adorned with patience, ^18-168

he is the foundation of all the treasures of merit, ^18-169

adorned with all the excellent minor marks, ^18-170

and the full flowering of the major marks, ^18-171

with timely and appropriate activity, ^18-172

with nothing displeasing to see, ^18-173

He brings complete joy to those who have faith and devotion, ^18-174

He has unconquerable wisdom, ^18-175

His powers are invincible, ^18-176

He is the teacher of all sentient beings, ^18-177

the father of all bodhisattvas, ^18-178

the king of all noble beings, ^18-179

the guide who leads all beings to the city of transcending sorrow, ^18-180

with measureless original awareness, ^18-181

with inconceivable fearlessness, ^18-182

with completely pure speech in melodious tones, ^18-183

one is never tired of seeing him, ^18-184

his embodied form is incomparable, ^18-185

unobscured by the realm of desire, ^18-186

unclothed by the realm of form, ^18-187

not adulterated with the formless realm, ^18-188

completely liberated from all suffering, ^18-189

totally liberated from all the aggregates, ^18-190

not encumbered by the elements, ^18-191

in complete control of the sense processes, ^18-192

He has completely severed all the knots, ^18-193

He is completely liberated from all agony, ^18-194

He is liberated from existence, ^18-195

he has crossed over the river, ^18-196

He has completely perfected original awareness ^18-197

(abiding in) all the past and future ^18-198

and present Buddha Bhagavan’s original awareness, ^18-199

not abiding in Nirvana, ^18-200

He abides at the limit of perfection itself, ^18-201

abiding on the level where all sentient beings are seen, ^18-202

all these are the authentic and supreme qualities of the embodied form of the Buddha Bhagavan. ^18-203

The holy Dharma is good at the beginning, good in the middle and good at the end, ^18-204

It is excellent in meaning, in words, and in syllables, ^18-205

It is unadulterated, utterly complete, absolutely pure, and absolutely purifying, ^18-206

it is taught perfectly by the Accomplished Transcendent Conqueror. ^18-207

it brings perfect vision, ^18-208

It is unafflicted, uninterrupted, trustworthy to apply, ^18-209

encountering it fulfills all aspirations, ^18-210

It is validated through the individual experience of the wise, ^18-211

the Dharma taught by the Bhagavan consists entirely on taming the mind, ^18-212

it is the arising of certainty, ^18-213

causing one to arrive at complete enlightenment. ^18-214

It is without contradiction, including everything, ^18-215

stable and unmoving, ^18-216

as for the Mahayana Sangha, they abide excellently, ^18-217

they abide with awareness, abide with honesty, abide in harmony, ^18-218

They are worthy of veneration with folded hands, ^18-219

worthy of veneration by prostration, ^18-220

they are a glorious field of merit, ^18-221

offering to them brings great purification, ^18-222

they are an object for generosity, ^18-223

they are absolutely the greatest object for generosity. ^18-224

<small>The sutra of remembering the Noble Three Jewels is complete.</small> ^18-225

The qualities of the Buddha are inconceivable,
the qualities of the Dharma are inconceivable,
and the qualities of the Noble Sangha are inconceivable.
By placing faith in that which is inconceivable, ^18-226

the ripening fruition is likewise inconceivable—
may all beings be reborn in the completely pure realm. ^18-227

When you, the Supreme among two-legged beings were born,
you took seven steps upon this great earth and declared,
You spoke: ‘I am supreme in this world.’
At that moment, wise sage, I pay homage to you. ^18-228

First, you descended from the heavenly realm of Tushita
and in Rajgir, you entered your mother’s womb.
In the grove of Lumbini, the Mighty Sage was born;
I pay homage to the Bhagavan, the God of Gods. ^18-229

In the celestial palace, you were served by eight nursemaids,
and among the Shakyas, you engaged in youthful play.
In the city of Kapilavastu, you took Yashodhara as your consort;
I pay homage to your body, unrivaled across the three realms. ^18-230

Showing the manner of disillusionment at the four gates of the city,
you cut your hair before the Pure Stupa.
On the banks of the Nairanjana River, you engaged in austere practices;
I pay homage to you who are free from the defects of the two obscurations. ^18-231

In Rajgir, you subdued the mad elephant.
In Vaishali, the monkey offered you honey.
In Magadha, the Sage attained full awakening;
I pay homage to you whose blazing primordial wisdom knows all. ^18-232

In Varanasi, you turned the Wheel of Dharma.
In the Jeta Grove, you displayed great miracles.
Beneath the twin Sal trees, you passed into final nirvana;
I pay homage to your mind, which is like the sky. ^18-233

The Blessed One, the master of this teaching,
By the virtue of praising the lord of the teaching,
Through virtue, may the conduct of all living beings,
become equal to the deeds of the Sugata himself. ^18-234

Whatever your body is like, O Tathagata,
whatever your retinue, the span of your life,
Whatever your excellent, supreme marks are like,
may I and all others become precisely like that. ^18-235

By the power of praising and supplicating you,
in whatever direction or land I and others dwell,
May illness, poverty, and conflict be pacified,
And may the Dharma and auspiciousness flourish, I pray! ^18-236

May the Buddha appear in this world,
May his teachings be as clear and bright as the sun rays,
May the study and practice of the holders of the teachings – masters and disciples be expanded,
May there be auspiciousness to remain the teachings forever! ^18-237

I bow my head at the feet of Bhagavat, Thathagata, Arhat, the perfect and complete enlightened Shakyamuni Buddha ^18-238

Bowing down the head ^18-239

I make offerings and take refuge! ^18-240

<small>Toss the flowers. Join your palms together and do the same here in after:</small> ^18-241

Please bless us! ^18-242

Unequaled and insatiable with a glance,
Beautiful golden form,
One face and two arms sitting in cross-legged posture,
Sitting with earth-touching posture, to you I pay homage!
Bless us to spread the teachings! ^18-243

Through the power of your prayers and aspirations are profound and vast,
The glorious who appears as the crest of all the victors,
The Lord who turns the wheel of dharma of the Three Yanas,
The completely enlightenment, to you I bow down with all my possessions! ^18-244

Although the virtues and possessions of samsara and nirvana are combined into one,
Not even a single hair pore can be endured,
The five wheels of adornmed acquired from hundreds of virtuous actions,
Completely realized by you, the teacher, to you I pay homage! ^18-245

The teachings of scriptures and realizations spread throughout all directions, and always –
May the holders of the teachings live a firm and long life and spread their activities!
Come to the glory of benefit and happiness for beings equal to the sky
Also, may all swiftly attain the unsurpassable wisdom! ^18-246

The only medicine for the suffering of beings
Ang the source of all bliss,
Gaining and venerating the teachings
Together with this, may the teachings remain for a long time! ^18-247

The only source of benefit and bliss,
May the teachings remain for a long time
And great beings who hold the teachings,
May they remain firm and long! ^18-248

May the Buddhas appear in the world,
May the teachings be as clear as the sun rays,
May the study and practice of the holders of the teachings – masters and disciples be expanded,
And may there be auspiciousness to remain the teachings for a long time! ^18-249

With beautiful flowers and beautiful garlands,
with beautiful music and perfume, and the best of parasols,
with the brightest lamps and finest incense,
I make offerings to every Victorious One. ^18-251

arranged in perfect symmetry,
I make offerings to every Victorious One. ^18-252

with devotion to all the Victorious Ones,
and empowered by my confidence in virtuous conduct,

The afflictions in the minds of beings like expelling the fierce beast of prey,
The deterioration of samsara and nirvana, protecting from the fear of bees, the heart tail whisk that slowly flutters,
Holding a ritual vase filled with nectar that the dust arising of self-cherishing is moistened by the enlightened mind (bodhichitta), ^18-487

To respect and service the assembly of aryas who take on a burden of benefit and bliss of the teachings and beings,
Completely surrounded by the assembly of Upasaka and carrying many scriptures of the three scriptural collections,
These beings, I and others, equal to the sky, just like you, I make offerings to the victors together with disciples,
May this become only the lamp of dharma who leads all beings without exception to the state of enlightenment! ^18-488

The only medicine for the suffering of beings
Ang the source of all bliss,
Gaining and venerating the teachings
Together with this, may the teachings remain for a long time! ^18-489

The only source of benefit and bliss,
May the teachings remain for a long time
And great beings who hold the teachings,
May they remain firm and long! ^18-490

May the Buddhas appear in the world,
May the teachings be as clear as the sun rays,
May the study and practice of the holders of the teachings – masters and disciples be expanded,
And may there be auspiciousness to remain the teachings for a long time! ^18-491

<small>Torma offerings of the Four Great Kings:</small> ^18-492

OM AH HUNG <small>[three times]</small> ^18-493

NAMAH SARVA TATHAGATA BHYO VISHVA <small>etc. Bless it with the mantra and mudra of the Space Treasury.</small> ^18-494

OM DHRIDHASHTAYE. VIRUDAKAYE. VIRUPAKSHAYE. ^18-495

VAISHRAVANAYE SAPARIVARA IDAM BALINTA KHA KHA KHAHI. <small>[three times]</small> ^18-496

ARGHAM <small>[onward]</small> SHABDA PRATICCHAYE SVAHA. ^18-497

The powerful one donning the armor of exertion,
Perfectly protecting the teachings of the Buddha,
Of the four directions: East, South, West, and North,
To the Four Great Kings, I pay homage!
Bless to flourish the teachings! ^18-498

The color of a thousand stainless moon,
The five types of armor and lute of Vaidurya,
The sound turned into the praise to the victors of the ten directions,
Please pacify the harms of these beings! ^18-499

The Great Guardian Kings together with their retinues, please pacify all obstacles of ailments and demonic forcesof all beings arisen from the eastern gandharvas and create happiness! ^18-500

One who has a face with trembling of summer rainclouds,
Donning with vajra garment, corpse-raiser razor tongue,
The dense forest of demons isdestoryed,
You, hero, subjugate the enemies of the teachings! ^18-501

The great king Virudhaka together with his retinues, please pacify all obstacles of ailments and demonic forces of all beings arisen from the southern Kumbhanda and create happiness! ^18-502

A mountain like that of lotus ruby,
Realizing the beautiful stupa of the Buddha,
The only hero of the battle who holds a snake,
Conquer all the prosperity and well-being! ^18-503

The great king Virupaksha together with his retinues, please pacify all obstacles of ailments and demonic forces of all beings arisen from the western nagas and create happiness! ^18-504

Manasvin with one hundred thousand suns in refine golden color,
The victory banner that raised on the pinnacle of existence,
Bursting out the stream of whatever one needs from Nakula,
Please increase the pleasures and wealth of this existence! ^18-505

The great king Vaishravana together with his retinues, please pacify all obstacles of ailments and demonic forces of all beings arisen from the northern yaksha demons and create happiness! ^18-506

In the east, the kingdom endowed with dharma is guarded with power,
In the south, beings born as the noble lineage of Mahayana,
In the west is Virupaksha who hates the enemies of teachings,
In the north is Vaishravana, who dispels the poverty and grants whatever one wishes, ^18-507

Even the four classes of Great Kings together with their retinues,
Please accept this offering of Torma,
To all of us, the yogins together with retinues,
Grant us to be free from sickness, to have long life and power, ^18-508

To have glory, fame, and fortunate times,
And accomplish all the vast possessions –
Please bestow peaceful, enrichment, and so on –
The siddhis of karma to me! ^18-509

Protect me by those who are bound with samayas!
Accompany me with all the siddhis!
Please pacify the ailments of untimely death,
Demons and negative forces! ^18-510

Please get rid of bad dreams and bad signs,
And all the bad deeds and dealings!
Bring peace to the world, and usher in excellent years!
May grains and livestock evermore increase; ^18-511

May all happiness and goodness be ever-present;
And may all that we wish for be accomplished! ^18-512

<small>With this, recite aspirational prayer of noble conduct, aspiration prayer of Maitreya, aspirational prayer of the pure realm free from the dust, and so on, whatever is appropriate.</small> ^18-513

In that only pure mandala,
Those with knowledge and realizations and treasury of compassion,
Never err in terms of the eternal aspiration through remembering and thinking,
Having understood this, with faith, I always pay homage! ^18-514

I make offerings that I have in real and imagined ones,
