Homage to you, supreme vajra, supreme benefactor!
Homage to you, perfect at the end!
Homage to you, arising from emptiness!
Homage to you, buddha’s enlightenment! ^34-164

Homage to you, passion of the buddhas!
I bow down in homage to the desire of the buddhas!
Homage to you, buddha’s delight!
I bow down in homage to buddha’s joy! ^34-165

I pay homage to the smile of the buddhas!
I bow down in homage to the laugh of the buddhas!
I pay homage to the speech of the buddhas!
I bow down in homage to the mind of the buddhas! ^34-166

Homage to you, arisen from nothing!
Homage to you, arisen from the buddhas!
Homage to you, arisen from space!
Homage to you, arisen from original awareness! ^34-167

Homage to you, net of illusion!
Homage to you, who shows the display of the buddhas!
Homage to you, everything of everything!
Homage to you, embodiment of original awareness! ^34-168

OM SARVA DHARMA ABHAVA SVABHAVA VISHUDDHA VAJRA. ^34-169

A A OM AH PRAKRITI PARISHUDDHAH SARVA DHARMAM YADUTA. ^34-170

SARVA TATHAGATA JNANAKAYA MAKLU SHRI PARISHUDDHATA MUPATAYATI. ^34-171

A AH SARVA TATHAGATA HRIDAYA HARA HARA OM HUNG HIH. ^34-172

BHAGAVA JNANAMURTI VAGISHVARA MAHA PACHA SARVA DHARMA GAGANA AMALA SUPARISHUDDHA DHARMADHATU JNANAGARBHA A. ^34-173

Then, glorious Vajradhara,
pleased and delighted, with folded palms,
Then, to the protector, the conqueror, the Tathagata,
the Sambuddha, prostrating, ^34-174

with the Protectors, Lords of Secret Mantra,
Vajrapani, king of the wrathful ones,
and various others, all at one time,
He expressed these words of secret praise: ^34-175

“Protector! We all rejoice!
Excellent, excellent, what you have perfectly taught,
seeking the result of complete liberation,
for all beings without protectors, ^34-176

You accomplish the vast benefit of we all attaining
perfect complete enlightenment!
Just this, taught in the “Illusory Net,”
is the completely pure excellent path! ^34-177

with vastness, and profound openness,
meaningfully accomplishing the benefit of beings,
just this is the object of all buddhas.
Just this is taught by all perfect buddhas. ^34-178

<small>This completes the Bhagavan Glorious Jnanasattva’s perfect recitation of the ultimate names, spoken by the Bhagavan Tathagata Shakyamuni.</small> ^34-179

## 35. The Heart of Wisdom ^35-0

Indescribable, inconceivable, inexpressible Transcendent Wisdom
unborn and unceasing, the very nature of space,
the realm of your own self-knowing original awareness;
I pay homage to the mother of the Buddhas of the three times! ^35-1

In the language of India: Bhagavati Prajnaparamita Hridaya ^35-2

In the language of Tibet: Chom Den Day Ma Sherab kyi Parol tuChinpayNyingpo ^35-3

I prostrate to the Accomplished Transcendent Conqueress Transcendent Wisdom! ^35-4

Thus have I heard, at one time, ^35-5

The Accomplished Transcendent Conqueror was dwelling in Rajagriha at Vulture Peak Mountain, ^35-6

together with a great gathering of the community of Bhikshus ^35-7

and a great gathering of the community of Bodhisattvas. ^35-8

At that time, the Glorious One entered the meditation that expresses the reality called “Profound Illumination,” ^35-9

and at the same time, noble Avalokiteshvara, the Great Being, the Bodhisattva, while practicing the profound Transcendent Wisdom, saw in this way: ^35-10

he saw the five aggregates to be naturally empty. ^35-11

Then, through the power of the Buddha, venerable Shariputra ^35-12

said to noble Avalokiteshvara, the Bodhisattva Mahasattva, ^35-13

“How should a son or daughter of noble family learn, who wishes to practice the profound Transcendent Wisdom?” ^35-14

Addressed in this way, ^35-15

noble Avalokiteshvara, the Bodhisattva Mahasattva, said to venerable Shariputra, ^35-16

“O Shariputra, a son or daughter of noble family who wishes to practice the profound Transcendent Wisdom should see in this way: ^35-17

seeing the five aggregates to be naturally empty. ^35-18

Form is empty, Emptiness also is form. ^35-19

Emptiness is no other than form, ^35-20

Form is no other than emptiness. ^35-21

In the same way, feeling, intention, and perception, ^35-22

consciousness are all empty. ^35-23

Thus, Shariputra, all phenomena are empty. ^35-24

There are no characteristics, there is no birth ^35-25

and no cessation. ^35-26

There is no impurity and no freedom from impurity ^35-27

There is no decrease and no increase. ^35-28

Therefore, Shariputra, in emptiness, there is no form, ^35-29

no feeling, no perception, no intention, ^35-30

no eye, no ear, no nose, ^35-31

no tongue, no body, no mind, ^35-32

no appearance, no sound, no smell, ^35-33

no taste, no touch, no phenomena. ^35-34

no eye sense faculty up to no mind sense faculty, ^35-35

no consciousness, ^35-36

no awareness of phenomena, no mind consciousness faculty, ^35-37

no ignorance, no end of ignorance up to no old age and death, ^35-38

no end of old age and death. ^35-39

In that way there is no suffering, no cessation of suffering, no origin of suffering, ^35-40

no path, no Original Awareness, and no non-attainment either. no attainment, ^35-41

Therefore, Shariputra, since the Bodhisattvas have no attainment, they abide by means of Transcendent Wisdom.Since there is no obscuration of mind, there is no fear. Transcending false views, they pass completely beyond sorrow. ^35-42

All the Buddhas of the three times, by means of profound Transcendent Wisdom, fully awaken to unsurpassable, true, complete Enlightenment. ^35-43

Therefore, the great mantra of Transcendent Wisdom, ^35-44

the mantra of great insight, the unsurpassed mantra, ^35-45

the mantra that makes all inequalities equal, ^35-46

the mantra that supremely pacifies all suffering, ^35-47

should be known as truth, since there is no deception. ^35-48

The mantra of Transcendent Wisdom is said in this way: ^35-49

TADYATHA OM GATE GATE PARAGATE PARASAMGATE BODHI SVAHA. ^35-50

Thus, Shariputra, should the Bodhisattva Mahasattva engage in the profound Transcendent Wisdom. ^35-51

Then the Accomplished Transcendent Conqueror arose from that meditation ^35-52

and praised noble Avalokiteshvara, the Bodhisattva Mahasattva, saying, ^35-53

“Excellent! Excellent!” O son of noble family, thus it is. O son of noble family! Thus it is, ^35-54

One should practice the profound Transcendent Wisdom just as you have taught, ^35-55

and all the Tathagatas will rejoice.” ^35-56

When the Accomplished Transcendent Conqueror had said this, ^35-57

Venerable Shariputra and ^35-58

noble powerful Avalokiteshvara, the Bodhisattva Mahasattva, and ^35-59

and that whole assembly, and ^35-60

the world with its gods, humans, demigods, and spirits, all rejoiced and praised the words of the Accomplished Transcendent Conqueror. ^35-61

<small>Then recite seven times or as many as possible, then, after reciting the awareness mantra as many times as possible:</small> ^35-62

I prostrate to the Dharma! Namo! I prostrate to the Guru! I prostrate to the Buddha! ^35-63

I prostrate to the Sangha! I prostrate to the Great Mother Transcendent Wisdom! ^35-64

May these words of mine come true! ^35-65

Just as, long ago, Indra, the King of the Gods, by the power and strength of contemplating the profound meaning of Transcendent Wisdom and reciting its words, ^35-66

was able to avert all harmful forces of negativity and obstacles, so, in the very same way, ^35-67

May I also, by the power and strength of my contemplating the profound meaning of Transcendent Wisdom, and reciting its words: May all negative influences be averted! * ^35-68

May they be destroyed! * May they be pacified! * ^35-69

May they be completely pacified! * ^35-70

Everything that arises interdependently
is unceasing and unborn,
neither non-existent or permanent,
neither arising or passing away, ^35-71

without a single meaning or several meanings.
All elaborations and explanations are pacified.
To the sacred words of the Perfect Buddha,
To that Holy One, I bow down! ^35-72

Pacifying the eighty-four thousand classes of obstructors,
Separating from all harmful conflicting conditions,
Accomplishing all desires with everything perfectly in place,
May everything be auspicious excellent bliss! ^35-73

## 36. The Profound Dharma of the Glorious Taklung Tradition: The White Umbrella Practice Method That Protects All Beings ^36-0

Homage to the Victorious, Auspicious Glory! ^36-1

Protecting all beings from the sufferings of samsaric existence,
And granting the cooling shade of complete liberation,
To the supreme Guru and Mother White Umbrella,
Who are inseparable, I pay homage. ^36-2

<small>Refuge</small> ^36-3

I instantly transform into the Lord of Sages (Buddha Shakyamuni), golden in color, ablaze with the glorious major and minor marks. ^36-4

His right hand touches the earth (bhumisparsha), His left rests in meditative poise (dhyana). ^36-5

He sits in the vajra posture upon a lotus and moon seat. ^36-6

Wearing the three saffron robes, his light fills all world-realms. ^36-7

From his crown protuberance (usnisa), a white OṀ radiates into the sky before me, ^36-8

resting upon a beautiful mandala of a lotus and moon disk. ^36-9

Light rays emanate from it, making offerings to the Noble Ones ^36-10

and fulfilling the needs of sentient beings. ^36-11

Re-absorbing back, it transforms into the Bhagavati, the Queen of Great Knowledge Mantras, the White Parasol Crown-Protuberance of all Tathagatas, Unconquerable and Reverting All Harm. Her body color is brilliant white like the autumn moon. ^36-12

She has one face and two hands. Sitting in the vajra cross-legged posture, she completely tramples all demons and misguiding forces. ^36-13

She has three eyes, her fangs are slightly bared, and her expression is both heroic and alluring. ^36-14

Her right hand is extended over her knee in the gesture of granting refuge (varada/abhaya). ^36-15

Her left holds at her heart the golden handle of a white parasol topped with a blazing wish-fulfilling jewel. ^36-16

Her dark hair is tied in a topknot above her head, ^36-17

with the remainder flowing loosely downward. ^36-18

She is adorned with diverse jewel ornaments ^36-19

and wears heavenly garments of upper cloth and skirt. ^36-20

Her body, speech, and mind possess the unhindered power of wisdom-vajra. ^36-21

Empowered directly by all Tathagatas, ^36-22

served by all Bodhisattvas, venerated ^36-23

and praised by all Sravakas and Pratyekabuddhas, ^36-24

all World-Protectors bow at her feet and execute her commands. ^36-25

She possesses the unyielding, blazing mark— ^36-26

She is the Great Wide Vajra Goddess who holds authority over the mandala of the three realms. ^36-27

She is of a nature non-dual between the Commitment Being (Samayasattva) and the Wisdom Being (Jnanasattva), sealed by Vairochana. ^36-28

At her heart, upon a lotus and moon, stands a radiant white syllable OṀ, surrounded by the mantra rosary. ^36-29

From it, light rays stream into the ten directions. From their tips, a mountain of terrifying weapons and divine implements erupts, ^36-30

driving away all harmful spirits, obstacles, and misguiding forces beyond a perimeter of twelve yojanas. ^36-31

The earth, sky, and intermediate directions are bound by the mantra and its light rays. ^36-32

A vajra ground, fence, net, ^36-33

tent, and canopy are formed, ^36-34

surrounded on the outside by a raging wall of fire—a secure protective wheel. ^36-35

The ones to be protected enter beneath the crest of the Guru White Parasol’s canopy, ^36-36

completely sheltered by her hand gesture of granting refuge. ^36-37

<small>Conceiving this visualization, recite the extensive dharani, or short version:</small> ^36-38

OM SITATAPATRE APARAJITE SARVA GRAHANA TRASAYA TRASAYA HANA HANA HUNG HUNG HUNG HUNG PHAT PHAT SVAHA. ^36-39

<small>Zlog-’gyur The Reversion Ritual At the end of recitation, perform the reversing ritual:</small> ^36-40

From all the Tathāgatas, ^36-41

You arose from their uṣṇīṣas as the Lady White Umbrella. ^36-42

You are the Invincible Lady who turns back hostile forces. ^36-43

Together with the hosts of mudra deities and the guardians of the world, ^36-44

And all those endowed with fierce power and miraculous ability, ^36-45

Advance against the harmful spirits and obstructing forces, ^36-46

And perform the fierce enlightened activity of repelling and reversing them. ^36-47

Turn back all harmful schemes and adverse calculations of worldly existence. ^36-48

Turn back all armies and hosts of gods and spirits. ^36-49

Turn back all unfavorable divinations and astrological calculations. ^36-50

Turn back all bad dreams and evil omens. ^36-51

Turn back all unfavorable signs. ^36-52

Turn back all harmful intentions and harmful actions. ^36-53

Turn back all sorcery, curses, and harmful ritual attacks involving ritual daggers. ^36-54

Turn back all fierce magical assaults. ^36-55

Turn back the various harmful zor rites, spirit attacks, and mustard-seed zor rites. ^36-56

Turn back harm caused by earth lords and nāgas. ^36-57

Turn back the life-stealing harm of theurang and elemental spirits. ^36-58

Turn back all the deadly forces of Yama. ^36-59

Turn back all the deadly forces of Yama. ^36-60

Turn back all harmful acts of hostile non-Buddhist practitioners. ^36-61

Turn back madness and possession caused by gyalpo and sen spirits. ^36-62

Turn back harm caused by planetary spirits and celestial forces. ^36-63

Turn back all the tsho-zor rites of Zhang Zhung. ^36-64

Turn back all the ox-zor rites of Sumpa. ^36-65

Turn back all the horse-zor rites of the Bönpos. ^36-66

Turn back all harmful mantra-zor rites. ^36-67

Turn back all harmful arrow-zor rites sent by people. ^36-68

Turn back all curses and imprecations uttered by women. ^36-69

Turn back all four executioners of Māra. ^36-70

Turn back all corpse-spirits and elemental spirits. ^36-71

Turn back all blood-zor rites of the Mamos. ^36-72

Turn back the eighty-four kinds of evil omens. ^36-73

Turn back the four hundred and four kinds of disease. ^36-74

Turn back the twenty-one thousand classes of harmful spirits. ^36-75

Turn back the eighty thousand classes of obstructing forces. ^36-76

Turn back the three hundred and sixty kinds of primordial fright and disturbance. ^36-77

Turn back the fifteen great harmful spirits that afflict children. ^36-78

Turn back unfavorable periods connected with years, months, days, and astrological cycles. ^36-79

Turn back the danger of untimely death. ^36-80

Turn back every unfavorable and opposing circumstance. ^36-81

BHAGAVATE SARVA TATHAGATOCCHISHA SITATAPATRE SAPARIVARA AGHAM. PATYAM. ^36-82

PUPPE. DHUPPE. ALOKE. GANDHE. NIVIDYA. SHABDA PRATICCHAYE SVAHA. ^36-83

<small>Praise with Indian classical texts, the praises composed by Sangye Onpo, or in short:</small> ^36-84

Through the mandala of the Great Wide Vajra,
You protect living beings of the three realms from all torments,
Bestowing the fine, cool shade of benefit and happiness—
Noble Goddess White Parasol, confer supreme dharani blessings! ^36-85

<small>Pray thus. The wisdom goddess returns to her natural abode; the commitment goddess dissolves into one’s own heart; rest in luminous clarity. Know the torma offerings through the standard ritual manuals.</small> ^36-86

## 37. The Practice and Recitation of Usnisa-Sitatapatra: The King of Birds, Garuda, as a Steed for Traveling to the Realm of Sukhavati ^37-0

Namo Śākyamunaye! ^37-1

The supreme consort associated with Amitāyus is the White-Clad Lady.
This goddess is her extremely wrathful manifestation.
Her purpose is to bring about rebirth in Sukhāvatī, the Land of Bliss.
In order to bind the Mamo protectors and their companions under sacred oath, ^37-2

This goddess manifested from the uṣṇīṣa of the Buddha, the Sage.
Her profound purpose is to turn back sorcery and harmful magic.
It was not spoken from the Sage’s mouth; rather, from his uṣṇīṣa,
The sounds of the sūtra and dhāraṇī resounded; therefore it is called “Uṣṇīṣa.” ^37-3

Although this teaching belongs to Kriyā Tantra,
It can be practiced in a manner resembling Highest Yoga Tantra.
Jetāri, Vajrāsana, and others,
And many great siddhas practiced it in this way, making it easy to put into practice. ^37-4

Beginning with Garab Dorje and Mañjuśrīmitra,
And transmitted to Vimalamitra—this is the Nyingma tradition;
Together with the treasure teaching of Guru TrakthungNakpo,
The Indian scriptures of the Ocean of Sādhanas and the Hundred Sādhanas, ^37-5

And works by Meaningful to behold, Wangchuk Dorje, and others—
The essential meaning of these authoritative Tibetan texts has been gathered together.
This practice is easy to perform and possesses great blessing.
For turning back illness, harmful spirits, sorcery, magical attacks, and ritual suppression, ^37-6

It is like a vajra diamond sword.
If you wish to journey to the Pure Land of Sukhāvatī,
It is like mounting Garuḍa, the king of birds, and riding there.
Therefore, make this a regular daily practice. ^37-7

Aḥ! All phenomena are emptiness, beyond speaking, thinking, and description.
A White Umbrella marked with OṂ completely transforms, And instantaneously, complete upon recollection,
I arise with the samaya being and wisdom being inseparable,
As the Great Vajra UṣṇīṣaReversing Goddess. ^37-8

Of her thousand heads, the two hundred faces in front are white and gracefully beautiful.
The two hundred faces on the right are yellow, terrifying, and laughing fiercely.
The two hundred faces behind are red, scolding and threatening.
The two hundred faces on the left are green, peaceful, and compassionate. ^37-9

The two hundred upper faces are blue, fierce, and wrathful.
Each head bears a vajra mark and has three eyes.
Her body is white, and she possesses countless millions of eyes.
Her eyes are opened wide and glance fiercely like flashes of lightning. ^37-10

With five hundred right hands and five hundred left hands
She raises blazing swords that accomplish whatever is intended.
With her five hundred right legs extended, she subdues every source of fear.
With her five hundred left legs bent, she subdues all harmful spirits. ^37-11

She wears seventy million Buddhas as her crown ornament.
Above the crown of her head, seven white umbrellas revolve, one above another.
Every hair of her body blazes with rays of primordial wisdom.
She wears divine garments and the ornaments of peaceful and wrathful deities. ^37-12

At her forehead is Vairocana, at whose heart is a white OṂ.
At her throat is Amitābha, at whose heart is a red ĀḤ.
Below her heart is Vajrasattva, at whose heart is a blue HŪṂ.
At the center of her heart, upon a moon disc, is a white OṂ, ^37-13

Surrounded by a mantra garland whose light clears away obscurations and increases power and strength.
Her upper teeth are ten wrathful male deities, and her lower teeth are ten wrathful female deities.
Her facial hair blazes like a mass of fire, and her tongue is sharp like a sword blade.
At her throat are the sun and moon, while her own body blazes like a mass of fire.
Visualize all harmful forces being completely scorched and burned to ashes. ^37-14

<small>Even if the visualization is not firmly established in the mind, simply meditating on the focal object is sufficient;
by meditation alone, the suppressions, entrustments, verbal suppressions and the like are released, as taught by Guru Khrag-'thung Nag-po. It is important to recite this aloud.</small> ^37-15

HŪṂ! Great Vajra Uṣṇīṣa Reversing Goddess, ^37-16

Great Lady possessing one thousand hands, ^37-17

Great Lady possessing one thousand heads, ^37-18

Great Lady possessing hundreds of thousands and millions of eyes, ^37-19

You bear the blazing sign of the indestructible vajra. ^37-20

Through the great and vast vajra, you rule over the mandala of the three realms. ^37-21

Please protect us completely—ourselves, our people, our possessions, and all those around us—throughout both day and night. ^37-22

TADYATHA OM ANALE ANALE. VISHADE VISHADE. VAIRE VAIRE. ^37-23

VAJRADHARI. BANDHA BANDHANI. VAJRAPANI HUNG HUNG PHAT PHAT SVAHA. ^37-24

HUNG DRUM BANDHA PHAT. MAMA RAKSHA RAKSHA SVAHA. ^37-25

All buried curses, bindings, and ties upon the body, speech, and mind of myself and those to be protected— open, open! Release, release! ^37-26

All buried curses, bindings, and restrictions: SARVA APANĀYA SVĀHĀ! ^37-27

Pacify all evil: ŚĀNTIṀ KURU YE SVĀHĀ! ^37-28

<small>Recite this section seven times as the best, five times as medium, or three times as the minimum. Following that, recite the Heart Mantra and Near-Heart Mantra as much as desired according to your practice capacity.</small> ^37-29

OM SARVA TATHAGATA USHNISHA HUNG PHAT. ^37-30

<small>The Heart Mantra:</small> HUNG MA MA HUNG NI SVA HA ^37-31

<small>The Near-Heart Mantra:</small> OM MA MA HUNG NI SVA HA OṀ MAMA HŪṀ NI SVĀHĀ ^37-32

Through the merit of practicing this meditation and recitation, ^37-33

As soon as I cast aside this impure body, ^37-34

May I be miraculously born in Sukhāvatī, the Land of Bliss. ^37-35

Immediately after being born there, may I swiftly traverse the ten bodhisattva bhūmis, ^37-36

And through emanations throughout the ten directions, may I accomplish the welfare of others. ^37-37

Then look directly, vividly, and nakedly at the mind of the one who is meditating. ^37-38

Finding nothing whatsoever to see, rest clearly and openly in that very state. ^37-39

In daily practice, even if you cannot clearly maintain all the visualizations, ^37-40

Recite the two short mantras during all your daily activities. ^37-41

Alas! Because of the weakness of the merit accumulated in previous lives, ^37-42

It is like receiving a written demand for a debt when one has never consumed what is claimed. ^37-43

Although one has done nothing, one’s name nevertheless appears in a document directing harmful magic against one. ^37-44

One may fall under the power of hostile magical rites performed by various Buddhist and Bön practitioners. ^37-45

One may constantly be tormented by many illnesses and adverse circumstances. ^37-46

Although one remembers death and wishes to practice the sacred Dharma, ^37-47

One may lack the good fortune to practice the sacred Dharma properly. ^37-48

There is no certainty when death will come, and there is no way to turn death away forever. ^37-49

After death, there is no certainty where we will go—alas! ^37-50

Whichever of the six realms we are born into, there is great suffering. ^37-51

If there is anyone who keeps these things in mind, ^37-52

Make this instruction your constant personal Dharma practice. ^37-53

In former times, Dharma King TrisongDetsen ^37-54

Invited more than one hundred learned scholars from India, Tibet, and surrounding lands. ^37-55

They translated the teachings that became the Kangyur and Tengyur into Tibetan. ^37-56

At that time, when a protective rite was required for the king to repel harmful magic, ^37-57

Nothing more profound than this was found within the Kangyur. ^37-58

Therefore, this practice was performed as the king’s protective rite for repelling harmful magic. ^37-59

The Indian non-Buddhist practitioners and the Chinese Hwashang, ^37-60

And all the Tibetan Bön practitioners, were unable to harm him. ^37-61

Later, the Mongol Oirat king ^37-62

Constantly maintained this as his personal daily practice. ^37-63

The text says that although he destroyed the Karma Kagyu establishment, harmful forces were unable to injure him. ^37-64

Therefore, it is said that there is nothing more profound than this for repelling harmful magic. ^37-65

Furthermore, the meaning taught in this dhāraṇī ^37-66

May be summarized very briefly in verse as follows: ^37-67

The blessings and merit of countless millions of Buddhas ^37-68

Are said to be obtained by oneself through this practice. ^37-69

The obscurations of the five deeds of immediate retribution and all other karmic obscurations ^37-70

Will, without exception, be purified and cleansed. ^37-71

One’s lifespan, merit, and strength will greatly increase. ^37-72

Whenever the time comes to die and depart from this life, ^37-73

It is taught that one will be born in the Pure Land of Sukhāvatī. ^37-74

In this life adverse circumstances are turned away; in the next, one attains a pure realm. ^37-75

One reaches the irreversible state, establishing one’s ultimate spiritual destiny, ^37-76

And comes close to attaining perfect Buddhahood. ^37-77

In order that such a practice might benefit both oneself and others, ^37-78

Rāga Asya brought together the scriptural sources and oral instructions. ^37-79

The one called Padma Tharchin committed them to writing. ^37-80

If this contains incorrect mantra pronunciation, contradictions, mistakes, or other faults, ^37-81

I confess them before all those endowed with the eye of primordial wisdom. ^37-82

Through this virtue, may all obstacles of all beings be completely pacified. ^37-83

In their next life, may they be born in the Pure Land of Great Bliss. ^37-84

Having been born there, may they accomplish immeasurable benefit for others. ^37-85

<small>The White Parasol Reversal is as follows:</small> ^37-86

BhagavatīSitātapatrā, and your hosts of deities, ^37-87

Through your unobstructed power and strength, ^37-88

And the force of your compassionate aspirations, ^37-89

May you fulfil these prayers of ours: ^37-90

Cause the teachings of Buddha to flourish, ^37-91

And all beings to gain happiness! ^37-92

For our patrons and those around us, ^37-93

May tragedy and disaster be averted! ^37-94

May all bad signs in dreams be dispelled! ^37-95

May all inauspicious omens and circumstances be averted! ^37-96

May the malevolence and harm of enemies and hostile forces be averted! ^37-97

May the eighty-four evil omens be averted! ^37-98

May the three hundred and sixty forms of daily suffering be averted! ^37-99

May the four hundred and four types of disease2 be averted! ^37-100

May the eighty thousand types of obstacle-making forces be averted! ^37-101

May the eight kinds of untimely death be averted! ^37-102

May initial misfortune be averted!3 ^37-103

May persistent misfortune be averted!4 ^37-104

May fatal conflict be averted! ^37-105

May misfortune arising from mistreatment of ancestral spirits be averted! ^37-106

May sickness among men and women be averted! ^37-107

May loss in our resources be averted! ^37-108

May calamities in the atmosphere be averted! ^37-109

May calamities on the earth be averted! ^37-110

May unfavourable signs in astrological squares and trigrams be averted! ^37-111

May astrological hindrances and recurrent calamity be averted! ^37-112

<small>This practice for averting obstacles through Sitātapatrā was composed by DrupchenLekyi Dorje.</small> ^37-113

May all adverse circumstances be turned away!” ^37-114

<small>With these words, clap your hands. Composed by the great accomplished master Karma Dorje.</small> ^37-115

## 38. Sengdongma — the Lion-Faced Dakini ^38-0

I take refuge in the Buddha, the Dharma, and the supreme Sangha
until I attain enlightenment.
Through the merit I have gained by giving and other good deeds,
may I attain Buddhahood for the benefit of all beings. ^38-1

Om svabhava. ^38-2

Within the emptiness of all phenomena,
in the centre of a blazing mountain of fire,
upon a lotus and sun,
from Hūṃ I arise as a dakini: ^38-3

Lion-faced and black in colour,
holding a curved knife and a skull cup filled with blood,
and raising a khaṭvāṅga staff. I am adorned with a garland of heads and the five symbolic ornaments.
My three places are marked with Oṃ, Āḥ, and Hūṃ.
Light radiates out, invites the wisdom being, and dissolves her into me.
I receive empowerment, and Akṣobhya adorns the crown of my head. ^38-4

OM SHRI VAJRA DAKINI SAPARIVARA AGHAM <small>[onward]</small> SHABDA PRATICCHAYE SVAHA. ^38-5

Glorious Vajra Dakini,
sovereign of the dakinis,
whose body, speech, and mind possess the five wisdoms:
I bow to you and praise you, protector of beings. ^38-6

At the centre of my heart is a ten-petalled lotus.
Upon a sun disc at its centre is the syllable HŪṂ.
As the surrounding mantra garland revolves, light radiates out.
May illness, harmful spirits, and obstacles be pacified, and may adverse circumstances be turned away. ^38-7

AH KAH SAH MAH RAH CHAH SHAH DAH RAH SAH MAH RAH YAH PHATAH ^38-8

Wisdom dakini with the face of a lion: through the power of your deity, mantra, and meditative concentration, may everything harmful to us practitioners—to teachers and students, benefactors and those around us—be turned back. ^38-9

This includes hostile enemies, ^38-10

harmful obstacles, circumstances that interrupt our practice, ^38-11

sorcery and harmful rites directed at us from outside, and, in short, every inauspicious and harmful influence. ^38-12

May they be turned back. ^38-13

May they cease to exist. ^38-14

May they be pacified. ^38-15

May the hosts of blazing wrathful dakinis
subdue every enemy and obstacle.
May they reduce their bodies and speech to particles of ash
and guide their consciousnesses into the expanse of reality. ^38-16

May the eighty thousand kinds of obstructing forces be pacified.
May we be free from harmful and adverse circumstances.
May our wishes be fulfilled, and may everything be abundant.
Through this auspiciousness, may there be happiness and well-being here today. ^38-17

<small>It is said that this prayer was composed by Khamtrul Kunga Tenzin.</small> ^38-18

## 39. Long Life Prayer of the 14th Gyalwang ^39-0

OM SVA STI — may all be auspicious. ^39-1

You are the noble Avalokiteśvara himself, the supreme one who guides beings.
Protector of the Land of Snows, Tenzin Gyatso may your life remain firm and lasting.
In accordance with the intention of your stainless mind,
may you soon be enthroned in the Potala Palace,
and may the Buddha’s teachings flourish and spread. ^39-2

and may the Buddha’s teachings flourish and spread. In the land surrounded by a ring of snow mountains,
you are the source of every benefit and happiness.
Avalokiteśvara, Tenzin Gyatso,
may your lotus feet remain steadfast until the end of existence. ^39-3

## 40. A Long-Life Prayer Entitled “May the Aims of This Prayer Be Fulfilled” ^40-0

O peerless King of the Śākyas, teacher of the asuras;
O great holders of the treasury of the teachings,
who have appeared in succession from Vajradhara down to my root lama:
please let the nectar-rain of your blessings fall continuously. ^40-1

From the treasury of the heart of Phagmo Drupa, protector of beings,
came the precious Dharma instructions.
Holding these instructions, the successive supreme masters were empowered,
one after another, as guardians of the teachings. To them I bow. ^40-2

May your unwavering resolve to uphold the Buddha’s teachings remain firm.
May your power grow to practise and spread the essence of the Victorious One’s Dharma:
all the instructions on devotion and Mahāmudrā,
and the great tradition of the teachings of scripture and realization— ^40-3

Of the traditions in general, and especially of the lineage that transmits realization,
May your power to practise and spread its essential teachings grow strong
Through meditation on the meaning of the Three Baskets and the classes of tantra,
Through hearing and reflection, they attained the state of a learned master.
may you bring genuine experience to its fullest measure.
May you live long as a glory to the practice lineage. ^40-4

Through your strength, may the Victorious One’s teachings flourish—
especially the extraordinary great tradition known as the Taklung Kagyu.
May its supreme Dharma instructions, the Four Jewels Encircled,
spread in every direction and endure for a long time. ^40-5

By the power of the truth of the lamas, the hosts of deities, and the Three Jewels;
by the unfailing nature of reality and dependent arising;
and by the strength of our deep devotion,
may the aims of this prayer be fulfilled spontaneously, without effort. ^40-6

<small>The Buddhist monk Tenzin Gyatso composed this prayer, together with its opening verses, on November 10, 2000, so that TaklungShabdrung Tenzin Gyurme Chökyi Wangchuk Rinpoche might live long and his enlightened activities might flourish for the benefit of the teachings and beings.</small> ^40-7

## 41. Long-Life Prayer Called “May All Virtue and Goodness Increase” ^41-0

Lord of all, Vajradhara, who possesses the sevenfold union;
Tilopa, Nāropa, and the three—Marpa, Milarepa, and Dagpo;
root and lineage lamas, chosen deities, and the Three Jewels: may you bless this prayer.
May the ocean of refuge-objects grant auspicious things ^41-1

With an unwavering resolve to uphold the Victorious One’s teachings,
you raise the banner of the Dharma of universal goodness in every land.
You have the courage and sincere intention to do so without fear.
According to the needs and inclinations of the many beings to be guided, ^41-2

you bestow the fine nectar of the sutras, transmitted teachings, and essential instructions.
You have attained the excellence of learning and realization.
As a true support for the teachings and for beings, may you live long. ^41-3

To uphold the banner of the Taklung tradition— whose devotion has reached perfection,
and whose teachings are preserved through study and practice—
may you naturally possess every noble quality.
May you live long, while your activities arise spontaneously and extend everywhere at all times. ^41-4

By the profound power of the blessings of the Buddhas and their heirs,
and by the unwavering strength of the connection created through the devotion of myself and others,
may all that we pray for be fulfilled just as we wish.
May the light of virtue and goodness increase everywhere. ^41-5

Sarva maṅgalam may all be auspicious. How virtuous! ^41-6

<small>This prayer, entitled May All Virtue and Goodness Increase, asks that TaklungMatrul Tenzin Kunzang Jigme live long for the benefit of the teachings and beings, and that his activities flourish. The Taklung lamas and monks remaining in Tibet requested it through Gyaltsen Lungtok, whom they sent to serve the tulku and who presented the request with a ceremonial scarf. In response, the Buddhist monk Tenzin Gyatso composed it on August 2, 1989, corresponding to the first day of the sixth month of the Tibetan Earth Snake year.</small> ^41-7

## 42. Long-Life Prayer Called “The Sun of Speech” ^42-0

O Sun of Speech, supreme guide and crown of the Śākyas;
the seventeen great scholars and accomplished masters;
Tilopa and Nāropa; Marpa, Milarepa, and Dagpo;TaklungThangpa-
and all the lineage lamas: please grant your blessings of virtue and goodness. ^42-1

Buddha Amitāyus, lord of the victorious ones, moved by compassion for beings of the five classes,
you bestow the supreme gift of an indestructible life.
Please bestow here today your great blessing for the attainment of deathlessness.
Please bestow here today your great blessing for the attainment of deathlessness. ^42-2

Through the power of your noble aspiration
to uphold the sublime treatises of the wonderful Buddha,
you have become a source of goodness for beings living in this age of five degenerations.
O Chökyi Wangchuk, may your lotus feet remain firm. ^42-3

Without favouring only one of hearing, reflection, and meditation,
you live the exemplary life of one who is learned, disciplined, and good.
You preserve and spread the Buddha’s teachings, both in general and in your own tradition.
O peerless great holder of the teachings, may you live long. ^42-4

By the pure nature of dependent arising
and the power of the truth of the precious Three Jewels,
may every aim of this prayer
be fulfilled easily and without obstacles. ^42-5

<small>TaklungMatrul Rinpoche requested that, when the hair of the young reincarnation of TaklungTsetrul Rinpoche was cut in the customary ceremony, the child also be given a name and a long-life prayer. In response, the Dalai Lama gave the child the name Tenzin Chökyi Wangchuk and composed this prayer. He wrote it on the twelfth day of the fourth month of the Fire Bird year of the seventeenth Tibetan cycle, corresponding to June 6, 2017. May it be virtuous.</small> ^42-6

## 43. Long-Life Prayer Called “A Melodious Song for the Attainment of Deathlessness” ^43-0

Om svasti — may all be auspicious. ^43-1

By the extraordinary power of the aspirationsof the glorious Karmapa,
who embodies all the victorious ones,
and of the countless deities of the Three Roots, who embody his wisdom and compassion,
may the lotus feet of this supreme tulku remain firm. ^43-2

With the noble aspiration to benefit others alone,
may you continue to spread the joyful feast of the teachings of scripture and realization—
both the Buddha’s teachings in general and those of the glorious Taklungtradition in particular.
May the armour of your three secrets—body, speech, and mind—remain firm, enduring, and unchanging. ^43-3

May your activities bringing benefit and happiness reach throughout the world.
May you perfect the excellence of discipline, hearing, reflection, and meditation.
Through the good qualities of spontaneously accomplishing the two aims—your own welfare and that of others—
may you shine with beauty and clarity throughout the three worlds. ^43-4

<small>Many people requested a long-life prayer for the reincarnation of the noble master of MarthangRiwoche. In response, this was written by the Buddhist monk known as Thubten JangchubChökyi Nyima, who calls himself a teacher of the glorious Taklung tradition in these latter times. May virtue and goodness increase.</small> ^43-5

May the glorious lama live long.
May happiness and well-being arise for all beings, as vast as space.
May I and every other being gather merit and purify our obscurations,
and may we swiftly reach the state of Buddhahood. ^43-6

May the Buddha’s teachings, the source of all the riches of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa, endure for a long time.
May the noble people who uphold
those-Teachings live long,
adorned with every good fortune. ^43-7

I pray that the lama remain in good health.
I pray especially that the lama live a long life.
I pray that the lama’s activities flourish and spread.
Bless me never to be separated from the lama. ^43-8

May the teachings, the sole source of benefit and happiness,
endure for a long time.
And may the lives of those who uphold the teachings
remain as steadfast as a victory banner. ^43-9

## 44. Butter Lamp Prayer by the Venerable Lord (Atisha) ^44-0

Butter Lamp Prayer spoken by the Peerless Venerable Lord, Glorious Atisha ^44-1

May this butter lamp vessel become as vast as the billionfold universe! ^44-2

May this wick grow as large as the Supreme King of Mountains! ^44-3

May the butter oil become as vast as the ocean at the edge of the world! ^44-4

May a billion of these lamps appear before each and every Buddha! ^44-5

Their light shining from the pinnacle of existence down ^44-6

to the lowest hell, clearly revealing all the realms of Buddhas and Bodhisattvas in the ten directions! ^44-7

OM VAJRA ALOKE A HUNG. ^44-8

Emaho! This wonderful and amazing light, burning brightly, ^44-9

I offer to the thousand buddhas of this fortunate age,
and to all the infinite realms of the ten directions, without exception,
to the Gurus, Yidams, Dakinis and Dharmapalas,
and all the deities of all the mandalas, I make this offering! ^44-10

May all beings, particularly my own parents,
in this life and in all lives to come, wherever we are born,
directly perceive all the perfect buddha realms
and remain inseparable from the Lord of Boundless Light (Amitabha)! ^44-11

And by the power of truth of this aspiration prayer
to the Three Jewels and all the assembled deities of the Three Roots,
May this prayer be quickly accomplished! ^44-12

TADYATHA PANCHENDRIYA AVABODHANAYE SVAHA. ^44-13

<small>It is said that the Venerable Lord Glorious Atisha and seventeen disciples would chant this together as they made offerings in the shrines of Ü and Tsang.</small> ^44-14

## 45. The Pinnacle of the Victory Banner ^45-0

Like the sovereign king at the pinnacle of the victory banner,
The sole crown ornament upon the heads of the supreme deities
You bestow the supreme accomplishments upon practitioners.
May there be the auspiciousness of the inseparable Gurus! ^45-1

Great Vajradhara, Tilopa and Nāropa,
Marpa, Milarepa, and the venerable Gampopa,
DrogönChöjé, Thangpa, the spiritual father and sons, and the others—
May there be the auspiciousness of all the Kagyu Gurus! ^45-2

The unsurpassed Teacher is the Precious Buddha.
The unsurpassed Protector is the Precious Sacred Dharma.
The unsurpassed Guide is the Precious Sangha.
May there be the auspiciousness of the Three Jewels, the sources of refuge! ^45-3

Glorious Protectors Four-Armed Mahākāla, Six-Armed Mahākāla, Legden, and the Protector of the Tent,
The Dharma guardians Four-Faced Protector and the Goddess Düsolma,
The activity protectors Sogdü and the Great King Vaiśravaṇa,
May there be the auspiciousness of the wealth lords and yakṣas! ^45-4

The divine maiden Tashi Tseringma and her five sisters,
The divine lay protector Dorje Künkyob and others,
Who are devoted to the teachings and protect the side of virtue,
May there be the auspiciousness of all the local and territorial guardians! ^45-5

In former times, in the presence of the Blessed One,
We practitioners who uphold the practice,
You who have vowed to care for yogic practitioners as your own children.
May there be the auspiciousness of all the Dharma protectors and guardians! ^45-6

May the eighty thousand kinds of obstructing forces be pacified,
May we be free from all adverse and harmful circumstances,
May favorable conditions be accomplished and everything become excellent and complete,
And through that auspiciousness, may happiness and well-being prevail here and now! ^45-7

May there be the auspiciousness of enlightened Body, immutable like Mount Meru!
May there be the auspiciousness of enlightened Speech endowed with its sixty qualities!
May there be the auspiciousness of enlightened Mind, free from extremes and conceptual elaborations!
May there be the auspiciousness of the Victorious Ones’ enlightened Body, Speech, and Mind! ^45-8

May the lotus feet of the Gurus, the glory of the teachings, remain firm.
May beings who uphold the teachings fill the whole earth.
May the power and prosperity of the patrons of the teachings increase.
May there be the auspiciousness of the teachings remaining for a very long time! ^45-9

Through the blessings of Brahmā, Indra, and the guardians,
May the auspiciousness of the benevolent deities remain with us.
May all virtuous and excellent wishes be fulfilled exactly as desired,
And may glory abide and auspiciousness prevail continually, day and night! ^45-10

Whatever spirits or beings may have come here,
Whether dwelling upon the earth or in the space above,
May they always act lovingly toward all living beings,
And may they practice the Dharma both day and night. ^45-11

May there be happiness and well-being here by day and happiness and well-being by night.
May there also be happiness and well-being here at midday.
May happiness and well-being remain here continually throughout day and night.
May there be the auspiciousness of the Three Jewels, the sources of refuge! ^45-12

## 46. The Supreme Among the Great ^46-0

The supreme among the great, the unsurpassed Teacher,
Through the blessings of the victorious Dharma Lord, like the sun,
May the hostile forces of Māras, obstructors, and all that causes harm be pacified,
And may glory abide and auspiciousness prevail continually, day and night! ^46-1

The unsurpassed supreme Dharma is the truth of the nature of reality.
Through the blessings of the truth of the sacred Dharma, the nectar of immortality,
May we be free from faults and wrongdoing, and may our accumulation of virtue greatly increase.
May glory abide and auspiciousness prevail continually, day and night! ^46-2

The Sangha blazes with the glory of precious qualities.
Through the blessings of the Bodhisattvas, the children of the Victorious Ones who benefit beings,
May the enemies of afflictive emotions, suffering, and torment be pacified,
And may glory abide and auspiciousness prevail continually, day and night! ^46-3

Through the blessings of Brahmā, Indra, and the guardians,
May the auspiciousness of the benevolent deities remain,
May all wishes for happiness and goodness be fulfilled just as desired,
And may glory abide and auspiciousness prevail continually, day and night! ^46-4

## 47. The Supreme and Sacred Teacher ^47-0

The supreme and sacred Teacher, worthy of offerings from gods and humans,
I pay homage to the Buddha; may there be happiness and well-being here and now!
Whatever sentient beings move or do not move,
May all of them have happiness and well-being here and now! ^47-1

Peaceful and free from attachment, worthy of offerings from gods and humans,
I pay homage to the Dharma; may there be happiness and well-being here and now!
Whatever sentient beings move or do not move,
May all of them have happiness and well-being here and now! ^47-2

The supreme community, worthy of offerings from gods and humans,
I pay homage to the Sangha; may there be happiness and well-being here and now!
Whatever sentient beings move or do not move,
May all of them have happiness and well-being here and now! ^47-3

May there be happiness and well-being in this place by day and by night.
May there also be happiness and well-being at midday.
May happiness and well-being prevail continually throughout day and night.
Through the Three Jewels, may there be happiness and well-being here and now! ^47-4

Possessing perfect excellence, he is like a mountain of gold.
The Protector of the three worlds has abandoned the three stains.
The Buddha possesses eyes like the petals of a fully blossomed lotus.
This is the first auspicious virtue of the world. ^47-5

The supreme teaching perfectly taught by him is unchanging.
It is renowned throughout the three worlds and revered by gods and humans.
The sacred Dharma brings peace to all living beings.
This is the second auspicious virtue of the world. ^47-6

The sacred Sangha is perfectly qualified and rich in the auspicious wealth of learning.
It is a worthy field of offerings for gods, humans, and asuras.
This supreme community is the basis of moral conscience and glory.
This is the third auspicious virtue of the world. ^47-7

May the Teacher appear in the realms of the world,
May the teachings shine brightly like the light of the sun,
And may the teachers and disciples who uphold the teachings flourish in study, explanation, and practice.
May there be the auspiciousness of the teachings remaining for a very long time! ^47-8

## 48. Prayer for the Flourishing of the Teachings of the Glorious Great Taklungpa Practice Lineage ^48-0

Within this auspicious expanse of all that can be known, in saṃsāra and nirvāṇa,
Whatever glory and excellent qualities there are are gathered at your two lotus feet.
Taklungpa, by relying upon whose feet all attainments are easily bestowed,
To you, the lord of the Victorious Ones, immovable and unshaken, I bow my head in homage. ^48-1

Known as GyéDzé Dorje, beautifully adorned with the ornament of ethical discipline,
Whose meaningful renown is the glory that dispels the darkness within,
Whose rays of realization spread throughout the vast fortunate age,
To Sangye Ön, who brings relief and liberation, I offer this prayer. ^48-2

Victorious Ones dwelling in realms throughout all- pervading space, together with the Dharma they taught,
Bodhisattvas of the ten bhūmis, Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, and the teachings of discipline,
The Three Roots and all other authentic sources of refuge—
All of you who are valid and true, please come as witnesses to this prayer of aspiration. ^48-3

From all the scriptural teachings of the Sage, beyond mere words,
The Dakpo lineage reveals primordial wisdom directly and vividly.
May the teachings of the glorious Taklungpa, who perfected devotion,
Flourish greatly, and may the communities of the Sangha increase! ^48-4

The vows that restrain the faults of conditioned existence are the gateway to complete liberation.
Through the three trainings of ethical discipline, meditative concentration, and wisdom,
May the teachings of the glorious Taklungpa, whose pure conduct is stainless,
Flourish greatly, and may the communities of the Sangha increase! ^48-5

The seed that causes peace and happiness, eliminates misfortune, and leads to omniscience,
Is engaging for others’ welfare in the ocean-like practice of the six pāramitās.
May the teachings of the glorious Taklungpa, supreme among the children of the Victorious Ones,
Flourish greatly, and may the communities of the Sangha increase! ^48-6

The outer world and its inhabitants—all appearances, sounds, and thoughts—are primordial wisdom;
Within the mandala, they spontaneously arise as the deities in their various forms.
May the teachings of the glorious Taklungpa, supreme among Vidyādharas,
Flourish greatly, and may the communities of the Sangha increase! ^48-7

Beautifully clothed in the three trainings and the conduct of awakening,
The supreme treasury of the four jewels of profound meaning,
May the teachings of the glorious Taklungpa, whose life of liberation is incomparable,
Flourish greatly, and may the communities of the Sangha increase! ^48-8

May those who carefully restrain their conduct through discipline uphold their own Dharma tradition.
May they become greatly learned and properly establish a gentle and disciplined mind.
May the Sangha wearing the pure saffron-colored robes flourish,
Increasing beyond measure and becoming an ornament beautifying the teachings! ^48-9

In the three worlds, it is the source of every benefit and happiness without exception,
And the medicine that pacifies suffering together with its causes.
May the Buddha’s teachings in general and the Taklungpa practice lineage in particular
Blaze brilliantly at the very summit of existence! ^48-10

Through the compassionate blessings of the vast ocean of all the Three Jewels without exception,
Through the power of the words of those who speak the truth,
And through the swift and powerful activity of the vajra-bound oath-keeping protectors,
May every aspiration made here be completely accomplished! ^48-11

<small>This was written by Taklungpa Chöying Lhündrub. May it be virtuous!</small> ^48-12

## 49. The Prayer for the Teachings to Blaze ^49-0

Buddha Vipaśyin, Śikhin, and Viśvabhū,
Krakucchanda, Kanakamuni, and Kāśyapa,
Śākyamuni Gautama, the deity of deities,
I pay homage to these Seven Heroic Buddhas. ^49-1

In the past, for the sake of sentient beings,
Whatever hardships I underwent,
And through my giving up my own happiness,
May the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-2

In the past, for the sake of the sick,
I completely gave away my own means of livelihood.
Through this act of protecting destitute sentient beings,
May the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-3

Sons, daughters, and wife,
Wealth, elephants, and chariots,
And precious things I gave away for the sake of enlightenment.
Through that, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-4

I made offerings to Buddhas and Pratyekabuddhas,
To Śrāvakas, to my father and mother,
And to the sages and ṛṣis.
Through that merit, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-5

Throughout many millions of aeons,
I experienced many different kinds of suffering,
While seeking learning for the sake of enlightenment.
Through that, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-6

I maintained ethical discipline and spiritual vows,
And practiced austerities for a very long time.
I made offerings to the Buddhas of the ten directions.
Through that, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-7

In the past, endowed with diligence,
I remained constantly steadfast and overcame opposing forces.
For the purpose of liberating all sentient beings,
May the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-8

I constantly practiced the discipline of patience,
And when sentient beings, corrupted by the dregs of afflictive emotions,
Committed harmful acts, I patiently endured them.
Through that, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-9

I cultivated the meditative absorptions, the liberations, and the formless attainments,
And samādhis as numerous as the grains of sand of the Ganges.
Through the power of having meditated upon them,
May my teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-10

In the past, for the sake of attaining primordial wisdom,
I practiced austerities in forests,
And thoroughly studied many treatises.
Through that, may the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-11

Out of loving compassion, I gave my flesh and blood,
And completely gave away the necessities that sustained my life.
I even gave away my limbs and minor limbs.
Through that, may the Dharma tradition greatly flourish! ^49-12

In the past, beings engaged in negative actions
Were clearly brought to spiritual maturity through my loving-kindness.
I established them well upon the three vehicles.
Through that, may the offering and generosity of Dharma greatly increase! ^49-13

In the past, when I possessed skillful means and wisdom,
I freed sentient beings from wrong views,
And established them in the correct view.
Through that, may the Dharma greatly flourish! ^49-14

Through the four means of gathering disciples, I gathered sentient beings,
And liberated them from the fire of afflictive emotions.
I defeated the increasing forces of wrongdoing and negativity.
Through that, may I and my assembly remain for a very long time! ^49-15

I guided other non-Buddhist adherents
Across the waters of erroneous philosophical views,
And established them in the correct view.
May my followers always remain devoted!
May the teachings blaze for a very long time! ^49-16

<small>Through the power of the two accumulations gathered by myself and others throughout the three times—past, present, and future. May the teachings of the Victorious Lord Mangala Śrī, Blaze and flourish for a very long time!</small> ^49-17

To the farthest limits of the great ocean and the wealth- holding earth,
in the centre, above the World of Brahma,
May the wish-fulfilling jewel of the Taklungpa Dharma
flourish vastly and abide forever! ^49-18

May it be auspicious! Blessings! ^49-19
